# Quirks of the original

> **Machine-Maintained, Human-Directed Project**
>
> This project is developed and maintained primarily by AI coding agents under
> human direction and oversight. Agents implement code, write tests, maintain
> documentation, and assist with development workflows.
>
> Changes are subject to automated validation and human-defined review
> requirements. AI-generated code can contain mistakes, and passing tests does
> not guarantee correctness. This project is not affiliated with id Software or
> Bethesda.
>
> This register was assembled by agents from a machine sweep of the repository
> and has not been read line by line by a person.

This page is the authoritative home of the list of quirks of id Software's
linuxdoom-1.10 (the behavioural authority of this repository, commit
`a77dfb96cb91780ca334d0d4cfd86957558007e0`) that a careful reader would call a
bug, an accident or a surprise. One row is one quirk: the quirk in a plain
sentence, the C file and line that causes it, what a player sees or hears,
its status in this project, and whether a small change to the C would remove
it. **Nothing here fixes anything.** The register records the original; it
does not change what any note, record, comment, test or tool says.

It is not the home of support status. What the Rust engine supports, refuses
or declares stays in [COMPATIBILITY](COMPATIBILITY.md); each row's status
column quotes or cites COMPATIBILITY (or the investigation note or evidence
record that COMPATIBILITY rests on) and is a summary, not a second authority.
If a row and COMPATIBILITY disagree about support, COMPATIBILITY is right and
the row is stale. Milestone planning is in [ROADMAP](ROADMAP.md); the roadmap's
[limits, overflows and quirks checklist](ROADMAP.md#limits-overflows-and-quirks)
links here.

**Keeping it current.** A pull request that finds or reproduces a quirk of the
original updates its row in this file in the same pull request, and adds the
row if there is none (standing rule in [AGENTS](../AGENTS.md)).

## How this register was built

Built on 2026-11-21 (America/Los_Angeles) from a read-only sweep followed by a
merge. This is a statement about that day's build, not a live count.

1. **The sweep.** Nineteen sweep passes read the investigation notes, the
   evidence records, COMPATIBILITY, the frozen records under
   [validation/archive](validation/archive/README.md), the comments and test
   names of the Rust source and the tools, and the commit messages on master,
   and wrote 890 raw entries. Each entry names a quirk, the C line the
   document cites, and where it came from. Quirks are often not labelled
   "quirk", so the sweepers looked for any behaviour of the original that is
   described as a bug, an accident, an overflow, an undefined or stale read, a
   limit, an asymmetry or a comment that contradicts the code.
2. **The merge.** The entries were grouped by the C file they cite and merged
   by seven passes into 443 rows. Every C citation was re-opened in
   `linuxdoom-1.10/` by the merger. Entries that are not quirks of the
   original (plain design, faults of the Rust port, harness artefacts,
   aggregate list entries, documentation slips) were dropped and are listed
   in [Dropped, and why](#dropped-and-why).
3. **The assembly.** A script (kept outside the repository) turned the row
   files into the tables below with no row or source retyped, and the same
   quirk found by two passes under two C files was joined into one row that
   keeps every source: 23 pairs were joined, leaving 420 rows. The statuses
   were then checked against COMPATIBILITY wherever a merged row needed one
   status; a status that no document gives is written "not determined from
   the documents" and no wire number is ever invented.

## What "verified" means

For a citation, "verified" means an agent opened `linuxdoom-1.10/` at
the cited lines during the merge or the assembly and the lines hold what the
row says. It does not mean the row's effect on a player was observed: the
"what a player sees or hears" column comes from the documents or is evident
from the C, and says "not stated" when neither gives it. Two citations are
marked "not verified" in the C column because the merger could not open the
line (a header or a function in a file it did not open). The last step of the
assembly re-opened the C for a sample of rows chosen across every file and
every cross-chunk merged row; the result of that sample is in the pull
request, not here, because it is a one-time observation.

## What was not read in full

Say it plainly: this register is only as complete as its sources and the
sweeps, and the sweeps did not read everything.

- **Documents.** Read in full: COMPATIBILITY, ROADMAP, the M5B, M5C, M4A, M4B
  and M4P notes and evidence records, the M3H1-M3H9C notes and records (the
  large timing and per-slot tables and the `*_IWAD_COUNTS` per-slot rows were
  skimmed, not read row by row), and the M2, M3A-M3G notes and records (the
  evidence records for M3F2-M3G10 and the ZSTD maintenance record were read in
  full by a second pass). The M5A note and record, ARCHITECTURE,
  ACTOR_IDENTITIES and VALIDATION were read in two passes that together cover
  every line. WEAPON_FORMULAS, ASSETS, EVIDENCE_STORAGE and README were read in
  full.
- **The archive.** The archived guides and notes under
  [validation/archive](validation/archive/README.md) were read in full except
  the manifest (headings and digests only), the `.txt` twins of the `.md`
  copies (not read separately; they differ in banners and links), and the
  data files (`.json`, `.csv`, `.txt` evidence under the `m3g*` and
  `maintenance-zstd` subdirectories), which hold no prose.
- **Rust source and fuzz targets.** Only comments and test names were read,
  never the code bodies. In `crates/doom-sim` roughly 5,600 comment lines that
  had no C citation and no keyword were read in a second pass; the generated
  tables `identity/names.rs` and `fine_sine.rs` were not read. In
  `doom-game`, `doom-play`, `doom-render`, `doom-sound` and the fuzz targets
  the first pass read a keyword-filtered extract plus every test name, and a
  second pass read the comment lines the first had not; "quirks stated in
  unlabelled prose with none of the filter words may have been missed" is the
  sweepers' own limit. A quirk mentioned only in code, with no comment or test
  name, would not appear here.
- **Tools.** The comments and docstrings of 371 files under `tools/` were read
  (about 9,400 lines kept after dropping empty ones); the code bodies, the two
  `source_suite*.json` data files and the pure data tables were not read.
- **Commit messages.** The sweep covered the whole history of master. The
  newest 150 commits were read in full by one pass; the same pass read the older
  ones of the 898 commits in the local clone only through a filter (a C
  citation, or one of the words quirk, undefined, overflow, uninitialised,
  stale, bug, surprise, accident, "does not match", contradict, `I_Error`,
  crash, "the original/genuine engine") plus every subject line, and a second
  pass then re-read every subject and body from commit 128 to the end of that
  log in full. The local clone is shallow (its log ends at the merge of PR #39),
  so a third pass read the 325 older commits, back to the original release, from
  a full log (almost all subject-only). The first 127 commits of the 898 were
  read in full by the first pass only.
- **STATE_FORMAT and TESTING.** Searched with a keyword grep only (quirk,
  undefined, overflow, uninitialised, stale, dangling, freed, accident,
  out of bounds, `I_Error`, never reset, zero-fill), not read. Every hit is
  either a Rust policy or already a row (the never-reset wall-step globals, the
  zero-filled mover allocations, the diagonal-wall reading quirks, the THING
  angle overflow); the grep found no new quirk.
- **C citations.** A few C lines were not re-opened by the merge and say so in
  the row's C column or notes: the X11 header value behind `Button1Mask`, the
  body of `P_ArchiveSpecials`, `p_sight.c:300-347` for one row, the zeroing in
  `p_telept.c` for one row, the SIGINT path of one row, `r_draw.c:843-875`, the
  lazy free and `s_sound.c` lines cited by one note, and `r_main.c:299-300`.
- **Not yet in the register.** The quirk list of milestone M6a ("U8") is not
  in yet; it goes in as rows when pull request #65 lands, and #65 is not edited
  for it.

## How to read a row

- **Id.** `Q-<file>-<line>` is stable; a later pull request points at a row by
  its id, written in text. Each row carries an HTML anchor of its id, so
  `QUIRKS.md#Q-p_map-189` works in a browser, but the repository's
  `tools/check_docs.py` resolves only heading anchors and so cannot check such a
  link. A second row on the same line takes a `-2` suffix. Paths of Rust
  source in the sources column are written without the leading `crates/`.
- **C file and line.** The lines were opened in `linuxdoom-1.10/`. A document
  that cites a different line is noted under the table of the file (or in
  "Where the documents disagree" when the difference is the point).
- **Status.** One of these words first, then the document's own words:
  - `reproduced (...)` the Rust engine does what the C does, under the wire or
    milestone named (the wire numbers are the policy table of
    [COMPATIBILITY](COMPATIBILITY.md#policies)); where a quirk is reproduced
    under some wires and refused under others, the row says both;
  - `refused (...)` the Rust engine does not run the situation: it rejects the
    map at load, or refuses the tic with a named error or witness and the
    state preserved, instead of doing what the C does;
  - `not yet ported` the behaviour belongs to a system the project does not
    have yet, or to a mechanism not admitted under any wire;
  - `not determined from the documents` no document says what the project does
    with it; nothing is guessed;
  - `not reproduced (declared ...)` a document states that the project
    deliberately does something else (a declared contract of the playable
    build, for example); the document is quoted.
  A few rows start with another phrase (for example "for the admitted types
  ..."); those say in their own words which case is which.
- **Small C change removes it?** `yes`, `no` or `unknown`, with at most a few
  words of why. This is a judgement from reading the C, not a claim that the
  change is safe: a "yes" never means the project should make it.
- **Sources.** The documents the row came from. Abbreviations: `COMPAT` is
  [COMPATIBILITY](COMPATIBILITY.md); `M5A`, `M5B`, `M5C`, `M4A`, `M4B` (a bare
  one, or "note") are the milestone notes such as
  [M5B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md](M5B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md); "record" or
  `VAL-M5A` and similar are the evidence records such as
  [validation/M5B.md](validation/M5B.md); `§` numbers and decision labels such
  as `S4`, `R3`, `T11` or `H3` are the note's own. "(line from the sweep)"
  marks a line number the sweep supplied for a document that names only the
  file. Source files, tools and commits are cited by path and line or SHA.

## The rows

### d_main.c

| Quirk | C file and line | What a player sees or hears | Status | Small C change removes it? | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="Q-d_main-150"></a>**Q-d_main-150** D_PostEvent stores the event and advances the head modulo 64 without testing it against the tail, so 64 unprocessed events make the ring read as empty (all lost) and more than 64 overwrite the oldest. | d_main.c:150-154 | a burst of 64 or more key or mouse events in one tic is lost; a lost key-up leaves its key held down. | reproduced (M5b: the genuine driver's `q` rows "(queue losses reproduced)" and the Rust ring's golden-pinned tests); the app itself posts at most 63 events per build, so it never reaches the loss. | yes: test head against tail before storing | M5B note 1.1.1 fact 3, 2.2.4 "The event queue"; M5B record step 4; [COMPATIBILITY#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input) ("at most 63 events per build"); doom-game/src/input/queue.rs:10-14, layer.rs:21-22, tests/m5b_mutants.rs:410-413 (test sixty_four_posts_wrap_the_head_onto_the_tail_and_read_as_empty); tools/reference/m5b_input_driver.c:43, m5b_input_scripts.py:228 |
| <a id="Q-d_main-153"></a>**Q-d_main-153** `eventhead = (++eventhead)&(MAXEVENTS-1)` modifies the variable twice without a sequence point, which is undefined in C89/C99, and the same form advances eventtail. | d_main.c:153, d_main.c:170, d_net.c:464 | nothing observable in play (gcc computes the intended `(x+1)&63` at -O0 and -O2, per the note's probe). | not determined from the documents (M5B note 2.2.5 item 6 records only that "GCC computes the evident value") | yes: write `(eventhead+1)&(MAXEVENTS-1)` | M5B note 1.1.9 fact 38, 2.2.5 item 6 |
| <a id="Q-d_main-166"></a>**Q-d_main-166** in commercial mode with no MAP01 lump D_ProcessEvents returns before advancing eventtail, so events pile up and wrap exactly as in the overflow case. | d_main.c:165-168 | not stated (commercial store-demo build only). | not yet ported (Episode 1 only; recorded as a source fact only) | yes: drain the queue before returning | M5B note 1.1.1 fact 4 |
| <a id="Q-d_main-274"></a>**Q-d_main-274** D_Display uploads PLAYPAL only when the game state changes to something other than GS_LEVEL, and ST_doPaletteStuff only on a change from st_palette, so a pure -warp or -record start never uploads palette 0 until the first damage or bonus palette. | d_main.c:274-275; st_stuff.c:1045-1050, 1456-1464 | possibly wrong colours at the start of a warp session (an X PseudoColor artefact; inferred). | not reproduced (declared): M5B note S13 "PLAYPAL 0 at start" (presentation only) | yes: upload the palette at level start | M5B note 1.3.5 item 2, 1.3.7 item 7, 1.3.8 question 6, 4 S13 |
| <a id="Q-d_main-331"></a>**Q-d_main-331** D_Display runs the whole screen melt in its own loop with no NetUpdate, event pump or tic, so the wall-clock time of the melt is dropped, the input queued meanwhile lands in the first command after it, and the next pass of the loop then runs up to 5 tics back to back. | d_main.c:223-229, 326-344; d_net.c:648-650, 669-677, 736-766 | the game stalls for about a second at level start and then catches up with a burst of tics; input is not sampled during the melt. | not reproduced (declared): [COMPATIBILITY](COMPATIBILITY.md) "The level-start wipe (decision T6)": "no melt picture is shown and the wipe takes no time"; the M5b profile is the declared "sound-free, wipe-free" contract (S15). | unknown | M5B note 1.3.4 items 4 and 17, 1.3.6, 3.8 "Wipe", 4 S15; M5C note 1.4.4 items 5-6, 1.4.7(b) item 4, 1.4.10; [COMPATIBILITY#the-level-st…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-level-start-wipe-decision-t6) |
| <a id="Q-d_main-331-2"></a>**Q-d_main-331-2** because the melt loop runs inside D_Display, I_UpdateSound and I_SubmitSound are not reached, so the in-process mixer is frozen and the device receives nothing for the whole wipe. | d_main.c:331-344, 395-405 | sound stops for the length of the level-start melt in the in-process build (the note says so by reading; not observed on a device). | not reproduced (declared): the sound schedule ties mixing to tics (docs/COMPATIBILITY.md "The declared sound schedule (decision T5)") and the wipe "takes no time" (T6). | yes: mix inside the wipe loop | M5C note 1.3.6 items 28.4 and 30.5; [COMPATIBILITY#the-declared…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-declared-sound-schedule-decision-t5) |
| <a id="Q-d_main-337"></a>**Q-d_main-337** the wipe's wait exits on any nonzero tic count, including a negative one if gettimeofday steps back, and wipe_doMelt's `while (ticks--)` then counts down through signed wrap-around (undefined past INT_MIN), about 4e9 passes. | d_main.c:333-337; f_wipe.c:188 | a long stall if the system clock steps backwards during a wipe (inferred). | not determined from the documents (recorded in M5C note 1.4.8 item 5 only) | yes: treat tics <= 0 as no progress | M5C note 1.4.8 item 5 |
| <a id="Q-d_main-392"></a>**Q-d_main-392** how many tics run per mixed 512-frame buffer depends on the wall clock and on the sound device's blocking write, so the same session produces different audio on different machines and runs, and the rate the device returns is ignored. | d_main.c:389-405; i_sound.c:770-781 | the same session sounds different from run to run (timing of starts against mixing). | not reproduced (declared): [COMPATIBILITY](COMPATIBILITY.md) "The declared sound schedule (decision T5)" ties buffers to tics ("not a claim about a session paced by a real sound card"). | unknown | M5C note 1.3.6 item 30; [COMPATIBILITY#the-declared…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-declared-sound-schedule-decision-t5) |
| <a id="Q-d_main-392-2"></a>**Q-d_main-392-2** S_UpdateSounds runs once per pass of D_DoomLoop, and a pass runs as many tics as the clock allows, so which sound starts find a channel can depend on timing. | d_main.c:389-392 | not stated (whether a sound is refused can vary with frame timing). | not reproduced (declared): T5 item 1 runs S_UpdateSounds "after every tic" (docs/COMPATIBILITY.md#the-declared-sound-schedule-decision-t5). | unknown | M5C note 1.1.7 item 3, 1.1.14 question 2; [COMPATIBILITY#the-declared…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-declared-sound-schedule-decision-t5) |
| <a id="Q-d_main-690"></a>**Q-d_main-690** IdentifyVersion picks the game mode from file names alone (doomu.wad retail, then doom.wad registered), so a four-episode WAD named DOOM.WAD runs as registered, not retail. | d_main.c:587-592, 690-702 | a four-episode WAD named DOOM.WAD gets registered-mode behaviour. | reproduced: the app takes "Game mode ... from the IWAD file name, as linuxdoom's IdentifyVersion does" (docs/COMPATIBILITY.md, M5b app; ROADMAP "partly done (M5b)"), and the wire 22-24 harnesses set `gamemode = registered`. | yes: identify the IWAD by contents | [M4B](M4B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) 1.1 (WAD header row) and Open items; [COMPATIBILITY](COMPATIBILITY.md) ("Game mode comes from the IWAD file name"; "Game mode of each wire's genuine harness"); [ROADMAP](ROADMAP.md) (game-mode row) |
| <a id="Q-d_main-885"></a>**Q-d_main-885** -turbo takes atoi of the next argument even when it is another option (so `-turbo -file x` reads 0 and clamps to 10), and above scale 254 the scaled forwardmove[1], which is also the MAXPLMOVE clamp, exceeds 127 so the command's signed char wraps. | d_main.c:885-902; g_game.c:171, 413-423 | odd command lines give surprising speeds; at extreme scales movement wraps (reversed or wrong). | not yet ported ("-turbo ... not started", [ROADMAP](ROADMAP.md); the turbo message itself is done) | yes: clamp the scale to at most 254 | M5B note 1.1.8 facts 36-37, 1.5.5 turbo row; [M4A](M4A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) 3.10.2 (G_BuildTiccmd ranges); [ROADMAP](ROADMAP.md) (-turbo row) |

Notes on the d_main.c rows:

- **Q-d_main-274**: the note labels the conclusion an inference.
- **Q-d_main-392**: the note labels it inferred, "not observed on a real device".
- **Q-d_main-885**: the wrap happens at the `cmd->forwardmove += forward` store (g_game.c:422), not in d_main.c, whose tables are int.

| <a id="Q-d_net-377"></a>**Q-d_net-377** the first TryRunTics sees as new tics the sub-second phase of the first I_GetTime call plus the time I_InitGraphics took, so vanilla's first displayed frame shows gametic 1 to 5 depending on the clock. | d_net.c:377-410; g_game.c:486; d_main.c:367 | the very first frame can be at a different game time between runs. | not reproduced (declared): M5B note S8 "The first frame shown is the one after tic 1, as in the harness" | yes: start the clock after initialisation | M5B note 1.3.2 "The first frame of a session", 1.3.8 question 5, 4 S8 |
| <a id="Q-d_net-381"></a>**Q-d_net-381** NetUpdate jumps to `listen:` without pumping events when no new tic is due, and the pass that hits the build cap pumps events without building, so mouse motion arriving during a long frame lands in one command. | d_net.c:381-382, 399-410 | not stated (a burst of mouse motion in a slow frame becomes one large turn on the first command of the batch). | not reproduced (declared): M5B note S4/S8, one accumulated mouse event per command build | no: inherent in polled input | M5B note 1.3.2 NetUpdate items 1 and 3, "Slow frames"; 1.3.7 item 4 |
| <a id="Q-d_net-404"></a>**Q-d_net-404** NetUpdate stops building once maketic-gameticdiv reaches BACKUPTICS/2-1, so at most 5 tics run per displayed frame and the clock time beyond that is never recovered. | d_net.c:399-410 (verified; the test is at 404-405) | after a long frame the game runs behind real time for good (a 30-tic frame loses 25 tics). | reproduced as a bound: M5B note S8 (i) "if more than 5 tics are due, run 5 and drop the rest (vanilla's bound)"; M5B record step 6 "5 due run 5; 6 due run 5 and drop 1; 30 late run 5 and drop 25" | yes: carry the excess to later frames | M5B note 1.3.2 "Slow frames", 1.3.7 item 3, 4 S8; M5B record step 6 |
| <a id="Q-d_net-669"></a>**Q-d_net-669** TryRunTics runs realtics+1 tics when realtics < availabletics-1, but only realtics when realtics == availabletics-1, an odd middle case between the two neighbours. | d_net.c:668-677 | not stated (one tic fewer than available in that case). | not reproduced (declared): M5B note S8 (i) uses its own monotonic due-tic rule; option (ii) "port TryRunTics exactly" was not taken | no: a pacing heuristic of the original | M5B note 1.3.2 item 4, 1.3.7 item 3, 4 S8 |
| <a id="Q-d_net-715"></a>**Q-d_net-715** the TryRunTics wait loop spins on NetUpdate and I_GetTime with no sleep, so the original burns a core while waiting; I_WaitVBL, which sleeps, is called only by D_QuitNetGame and the quit sound. | d_net.c:714-733; i_system.c:126-137; d_net.c:620; m_menu.c:1090 | not stated (full CPU use while waiting). | not reproduced (declared): M5B note S8 (i) "the loop sleeps until the next tic is due" | yes: sleep until the next tic | M5B note 1.3.2 item 8, 1.3.3 item 4, 1.3.8 question 2 |

| <a id="Q-doomdef_h-80"></a>**Q-doomdef_h-80** the comment says the integrated sound is "experimental, and unfinished. | <a id="Q-g_game-74"></a>**Q-g_game-74** a save larger than SAVEGAMESIZE (0x2c000) stops the game with I_Error "Savegame buffer overrun", and the length is tested only after the whole archive has already been written into the buffer at screens[1]+0x4000. | g_game.c:74, 1284, 1302-1311 | the game exits with an error when saving a very large level. | not yet ported (docs/ROADMAP.md#saving-and-loading: "not started") | yes: bound the writes, or a larger buffer | [ROADMAP#saving-and-l…](ROADMAP.md#saving-and-loading) |
| <a id="Q-g_game-127"></a>**Q-g_game-127** G_RecordDemo copies the -record name into `char demoname[32]` with strcpy and appends ".lmp" with strcat, with no length check, so a name over 27 characters overflows. | g_game.c:127, 1536-1537 | not stated (memory corruption or a crash with a long name). | not yet ported (the -record option is "not started", [ROADMAP](ROADMAP.md)) | yes: bounded copy | M5B note 1.2.1 item 3, 1.3.7 item 9; [ROADMAP](ROADMAP.md) (-record row) |
| <a id="Q-g_game-186"></a>**Q-g_game-186** mousebuttons and joybuttons point one element into their arrays so that index -1 ("no binding") is legal, but M_LoadDefaults stores every key and button binding from the config file with no range check, so an out-of-range binding indexes outside the arrays. | g_game.c:186-187, 203-204; m_misc.c:384-395 | not stated (undefined behaviour with a bad config file). | not yet ported (the app reads no configuration file, decision S7, [COMPATIBILITY#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input)); the Rust bindings type refuses an out-of-range value (`ControlsError`, crates/doom-game/src/input/controls.rs). | yes: range-check values when loading defaults | M5B note 1.1.6 "Default bindings and input settings" ("stored with **no range check** (m_misc.c:388-393)"); doom-game/src/input/controls.rs:9, 25-26, 173 |
| <a id="Q-g_game-264"></a>**Q-g_game-264** turnheld counts tics whenever a turn key is held, including while strafing and while both turn keys cancel, and tspeed stays at the slow 2 for the first five tics whatever the run key, so releasing one of two held turn keys gives the fast turn at once. | g_game.c:179, 262-275 | a turn begun while strafing or with run held starts slow; the slow start can be skipped by holding both turn keys first; a one-tic release restarts it. | reproduced (M5b step 4: `turnheld` is compared on every tic; test opposite_turn_keys_count_turnheld_and_cancel) | yes: count only an actual uncancelled turn | M5B note 1.1.4 steps 6-7, 1.1.10, 2.2.4 "Slow turn"; M5B record step 4; doom-game/tests/ticcmd.rs:165 |

| <a id="Q-g_game-1512"></a>**Q-g_game-1512** G_WriteDemoTiccmd stores only `(angleturn+128)>>8` and immediately re-reads the bytes into the command ("make SURE it is exactly the same"), so a recorded session turns in steps of 256 while an unrecorded one turns at full resolution, and a built turn of 32640 or more re-reads as -32768. | g_game.c:1501, 1506-1522, 667-669 | fine mouse turning is lost while recording: a turn of -128 to 127 units a tic is dropped without being carried, 128 to 255 becomes 256; the +32768/-32768 wrap is the same angle. | reproduced ("Recording round trip, and what it costs (decision S3)", [COMPATIBILITY#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input); M5b step 4 `record_round`; step 7b injects full 16-bit commands into the genuine runner); the opt-in `--unrecorded-full-turning` mode executes full turns and records nothing. | no: the demo format has one turn byte | [COMPATIBILITY#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input) and #unrecorded-full-turning-mode-opt-in; M5B note 1.2.2 items 2-5, 1.2.5 table, 1.3.7 item 1, 2.2.6, 2.7, 4 S3; M5B record steps 4, 7, 7b; doom-game/src/input/demo.rs:9-12, 23-25, 77; doom-game/tests/app.rs:854-857, 1227-1232; doom-game/tests/input_goldens.rs:223-226; tools/reference/m5b_inject.c:6-14, m5b_inject.py:4-10, 25-26, 82, m5b_input_scripts.py:130, 147-148; commits 2130d9c, b1f17e2 |
| <a id="Q-g_game-1512-2"></a>**Q-g_game-1512-2** because the stored byte is `(a+128)>>8` rounded toward minus infinity, the normal keyboard turn of 640 becomes +768 to the left but -512 to the right while recording (+128 becomes +256 while -128 vanishes). | g_game.c:177, 1501, 1512 | while recording, left and right keyboard turns at normal speed differ (768 against 512 units a tic); the mouse is "slightly lopsided". | reproduced (M5b step 2/4 goldens, "built-to-exec turns", every executed value a multiple of 256) | yes: round symmetrically | M5B note 1.2.2 item 2, 1.2.5 table, 2.2.6, 2.7, 4 S3 |
| <a id="Q-g_game-1515"></a>**Q-g_game-1515** once demo_p passes demoend-16 the recording ends through G_CheckDemoStatus and I_Error, so with the default 0x20000-byte buffer a session is cut after tic 32,760 (about 15 min 36 s) and the process exits. | g_game.c:1514-1520, 1538-1543, 1677-1683 | the game quits after about 15.6 minutes of recording. | not reproduced (declared): M5B note S21 "The app does not reproduce that limit; it bounds a session at 1,048,576 tics"; the genuine limit is reproduced in the recorder model of the input goldens (`demo_limit`: 32,761 commands); ROADMAP lists the buffer limit and -maxdemo as not started. | yes: -maxdemo already enlarges it | M5B note 1.2.2 item 7, 2.2.6 "Recording length", 3.6, 4 S21; M5B record step 6 and line 107; [ROADMAP#demos](ROADMAP.md#demos); doom-game/tests/input_goldens.rs:15-17 |
| <a id="Q-g_game-1559"></a>**Q-g_game-1559** respawnparm, fastparm and nomonsters hold M_CheckParm's return value, the argument's position on the command line, so G_BeginRecording writes for example 3, not 1, into the header. | d_main.c:808-810; g_game.c:1560-1562; m_argv.c:41-52; doomtype.h:34 | nothing observable in play (playback tests only for truth). | refused: "A header written by vanilla -nomonsters ... is refused" by the Rust reader (M5B note 1.2.6) | yes: store `!!x` | M5B note 1.2.1 table and "Quirk", 1.2.6 item 2, 1.3.7 item 11 |
| <a id="Q-g_game-1589"></a>**Q-g_game-1589** G_DoPlayDemo accepts only version 110, prints "Demo is from a different game version!" to stderr and returns with no level loaded for anything else, so the commercial 1.9 demos (version 109), including the IWAD's own DEMO1-4, do not play. | g_game.c:1587-1594; doomdef.h:33 | a 1.9 demo simply does not play in this source. | worked around, not ported: M3h9c uses its own version-110 demo ("A real 1.9 demo would need only its version byte patched 109 -> 110", decision G1); M4b patches byte 0 in a scratch copy (fact 34, I5); real 1.9 demo playback is "Later". | yes: accept 109 | [M4B#d-demo-versi…](M4B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#d-demo-version) (facts 31-35) and line 458; [M3H9C](M3H9C_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) §2.1, §11 "Quirks recorded" |

Notes on the g_game.c rows:

- **Q-g_game-579-2**: M5B note 1.1.9 fact 40 also records the negative division in the same expression as implementation-defined.
- **Q-g_game-660**: the M5B note marks the playback consequence "inferred ... it was not run"; the M5D record lists the C lines as checked on 2026-11-21.
- **Q-g_game-815**: The consequence for the menu pause is the register builder's reading of the M3H9C note's §2.7, not a documented effect.
- **Q-g_game-822**: deliberate per its comment; kept because two sweeps and the notes record it as a surprise for a player.
- **Q-g_game-1493**: The G2 sweep reported it as recorded only at M4B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md:1129; COMPATIBILITY and the M5D record record it too.
- **Q-g_game-1512**: the H1b and C sweeps cite the read as g_game.c:1500; it is at 1501.
- **Q-g_game-1512-2**: the 768/512 arithmetic was rechecked here from angleturn[] = {640, 1280, 320}; the fast (1280) and slow (320) turns come out symmetric.

| <a id="Q-hu_lib-154"></a>**Q-hu_lib-154** at screenblocks 10 and 11 viewwindowx is 0, so HUlib_eraseTextLine changes no pixels and the border redraw is skipped, and any view pixel the renderer does not write keeps old message-glyph pixels. | hu_lib.c:154-169; d_main.c:285 | message text can stay visible in hall-of-mirrors gaps after it should have gone. | reproduced (M5B note 3.8: the view is drawn over "the *composed* previous screen"; M5B record step 3 goldens match); a HOM room with a message is listed as a risk to test (M5B note 3.16 risk 2). | yes: clear the view area | M5B note 1.5.6 items 7 and 9, 3.8, 3.16 risk 2; M5B record step 3 |

| <a id="Q-i_sound-517"></a>**Q-i_sound-517** I_SoundIsPlaying is `gametic < handle` ("Ouch."), and the in-process build's handles come from a static unsigned short counter that starts at 100 and wraps after 65535 back to 100, so a channel counts as busy until gametic passes its handle whatever the sound's length: early in a session starts can be refused for want of a channel, later every channel is freed at the first update, and if more sounds have started than gametic-100 no channel ever times out. | i_sound.c:271, 334-340, 517-521 | early in a session a sound can fail to start for want of a channel, and later it cannot (110 of 26,077 starts of the 61 real runs found no channel). | reproduced (docs/COMPATIBILITY.md T13 row 9, wire 25, M5c; the mixer goldens include "the handle wrap") | yes: look the handle up in channelhandles | [COMPATIBILITY#audible-quir…](COMPATIBILITY.md#audible-quirks-of-this-source-reproduced-as-they-are-decision-t13) row 9; M5C note 1.1.6, 1.1.15, Summary, line 13; M5C record step 2c; doom-sound/src/logic.rs:7-9, tests/session_rules.rs:111, tests/logic_rules.rs:139; tools/reference/m5c_mixer_model.py:25-35, m5c_mixer_reference.py:14-18 |
| <a id="Q-i_sound-669"></a>**Q-i_sound-669** in the shipped SNDSERV build I_SubmitSound is still compiled and called each loop pass, writing the never-mixed zero mixbuffer to audio_fd, a zero-initialised global never opened in that branch, i.e. file descriptor 0. | i_sound.c:106, 665-670; d_main.c:402-405 | not stated (a write to standard input's descriptor each loop pass). | not reproduced (declared T2: every sound claim is about the in-process build, [COMPATIBILITY#the-referenc…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-reference)) | yes: guard the call with SNDSERV | M5C note 1.1.0 (table row for `#ifndef SNDINTR`, line 65), 1.3.1 (line 1763) |
| <a id="Q-i_sound-717"></a>**Q-i_sound-717** I_ShutdownSound prints "NOT finishing pending sounds", scans the channels once and sets done because its `if (i==8)` test is commented out, so it never waits. | i_sound.c:702-726 | sounds in progress are cut at exit. | not determined from the documents (M5C note 1.3.5 item 26; outside the port's scope) | yes: restore the test | M5C note 1.3.5 item 26 (line 2146) |
| <a id="Q-i_sound-770"></a>**Q-i_sound-770** when /dev/dsp cannot be opened I_InitSound prints "Could not open /dev/dsp" and carries on with audio_fd = -1, and the next myioctl then fails and calls exit(-1), so the in-process build exits during I_Init. | i_sound.c:159-175, 770-776 | the in-process build exits at startup without a sound device. | not determined from the documents (M5C note 1.3.7 B1: platform glue, stubbed by the harness) | yes: skip sound when open fails | M5C note 1.3.7 B1 |
| <a id="Q-i_sound-811"></a>**Q-i_sound-811** I_InitSound sets a linked sound's length to `lengths[(link - S_sfx)/sizeof(sfxinfo_t)]`, dividing an element index by sizeof again, so the chaingun sound linked to the pistol takes lengths[0], never written and 0, and would play one frame; the sound server has the identical expression. | i_sound.c:799-813, 332; sounds.c:204; sndserv/soundsrv.c:390; i_sound.c:801-812 (811); s_sound.c:283-294, 567-580 | nothing observable in play (no game code starts sfx_chgun; the chaingun fires sfx_pistol). | reproduced (docs/COMPATIBILITY.md T13 row 6; M5c saved state accepts a linked sfx_chgun not yet mixed; the independent mixer model refuses a start of it with an `o` row) | yes: drop the division | [COMPATIBILITY#audible-quir…](COMPATIBILITY.md#audible-quirks-of-this-source-reproduced-as-they-are-decision-t13) row 6; M5C note 1.1.13 item 11, 1.3.2 item 12 (lines 1890-1900), lines 960-961, 2297-2299; doom-sound/src/mixer.rs:166-177, src/lumps.rs:60, 71-72, 121, src/sounds.rs:47-48, tests/tables_and_lumps.rs:158, tests/state_restore.rs:459; tools/reference/m5c_mixer_model.py:21-23, m5c_mixer_driver.c:65-67, m5c_sfx.py:8-9; commit 24007da; [COMPAT#audible-quir…](COMPATIBILITY.md#audible-quirks-of-this-source-reproduced-as-they-are-decision-t13) (row 6); M5C §1.1.3, §1.1.13 item 11 |

| <a id="Q-i_video-102"></a>**Q-i_video-102** xlatekey reads keysym column 0 only (so Shift never changes the keysym) and returns Latin-1 keysyms 0xa0-0xff raw, where they collide with DOOM's own key codes (0xff is KEY_PAUSE, 0xbb..0xc4 are F1..F10, 0xac..0xaf the arrows). | i_video.c:102, i_video.c:152-157, doomdef.h:271 (KEY_PAUSE 0xff) (verified; the keysym values are X11 external knowledge, as the note says) | typing certain non-ASCII characters can open the menu or pause the game (observed in the genuine driver: 0xff gives BT_SPECIAL, 0xbb opens the menu). | not reproduced (declared, M5b decision S6 option (i): keys are "identified as the library reports them"); the genuine translation is evidence only (ROADMAP checklist "Keyboard and mouse events from X11": "partly done (M5b)") | yes: map only known keysyms | M5B §1.1.7 fact 26, §2.2.4 "The X translation" (table: 0xbb opens the menu), §4 S5 and S6; [ROADMAP#vanilla-comp…](ROADMAP.md#vanilla-completeness-checklist) (platform row "Keyboard and mouse events from X11") |
| <a id="Q-i_video-135"></a>**Q-i_video-135** Shift_L/R, Control_L/R and Alt_L/R/Meta_L/R each translate to one key code (KEY_LALT is defined as KEY_RALT), so releasing one side releases the action while the other side is still held. | i_video.c:135-150, doomdef.h:276-280 | running, firing or strafing stops when one Shift/Ctrl/Alt is released although the other is still held. | reproduced (M5b, decision S6: "left and right modifiers are one key as in the original") | yes: separate left and right codes | M5B §1.1.6 table, §1.1.7 fact 26, §1.1.10, §4 S6 |
| <a id="Q-i_video-152"></a>**Q-i_video-152** xlatekey returns every keysym it has no case for unchanged, so End, Home, Insert, the keypad digits, Caps Lock and the like give codes of 256 or more, which G_Responder silently drops (they are still eaten and still fed to the cheat matcher). | i_video.c:152-157, g_game.c:181, g_game.c:566, g_game.c:571 | End, Home, Insert, keypad digits and other unlisted keys do nothing in the game. | reproduced in effect (M5b, decision S5): "Keys linuxdoom's translation leaves as a code of 256 or more (keypad digits, Insert, Home and so on) are not forwarded either" ([COMPAT#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input)); the app uses End as its own mute key (M5c step 7) | yes: add cases for the missing keysyms | [COMPAT#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input) ("Never forwarded"); M5B §1.1.7 fact 26, §4 S5; [VAL-M5C](validation/M5C.md) (step 7, "the mute key End ... a keysym the game never sees, i_video.c:152-157, g_game.c:566-571"); doom-play/src/keys.rs:1-5, 14-19, 138 (test the_table_follows_xlatekey); doom-game/tests/input_goldens.rs:243-244 (comment); commit 8a19a88 "M5c step 7: doom-play plays the sound stream" |
| <a id="Q-i_video-189"></a>**Q-i_video-189** lastmousex and lastmousey start at 0, so the first MotionNotify is measured from (0,0) and reports a delta equal to the whole pointer coordinate. | i_video.c:189-190, i_video.c:250-251 | the first mouse movement turns and moves the player a large amount (observed: forward -50 and turn -5440 for a move to (170,110)). | not reproduced (declared, M5b decision S4 option (i)); observed in the genuine layer (`x_motion_first_jump`) | yes: initialise from the first event | M5B §2.2.4 ("lastmousex and lastmousey start at 0", `x_motion_first_jump`), §2.3.1 finding 1(c), §1.1.7 fact 30 |
| <a id="Q-i_video-197"></a>**Q-i_video-197** I_GetEvent builds a key event in an uninitialised local event_t and sets only type and data1, so data2 and data3 are indeterminate. | i_video.c:197, i_video.c:203-214 | nothing observable in play (no responder reads them for key events). | not determined from the documents (M5B says "never read"; the input driver prints `-` for them) | yes: initialise the struct | M5B §1.1.7 fact 27, §1.1.9 item 44; tools/reference/m5b_input_driver.c:125 (comment) |
| <a id="Q-i_video-203"></a>**Q-i_video-203** the X layer never turns auto-repeat off or asks for detectable auto-repeat, so a held key arrives as extra KeyRelease+KeyPress pairs, and a pair split across a tic clears the key for one command and resets turnheld. | i_video.c:203-214 (key events posted as they come; grep finds no XAutoRepeat call in i_video.c) (verified: the absence; the X server behaviour is the note's inference) | holding a turn key can restart the slow turn; Pause held would toggle pause repeatedly. | not reproduced (declared, M5b decision S6 option (i): "Key repeats are dropped", [COMPAT#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input)) | yes: XAutoRepeatOff or detectable auto-repeat | M5B §1.1.7 fact 34, §2.2.4 "X auto-repeat", §4 S6; [COMPAT#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input) ("The event layer") |
| <a id="Q-i_video-218"></a>**Q-i_video-218** the X layer ORs `state & Button1Mask` (bit 8, 256) into data1 instead of bit 0 while buttons 2 and 3 use `? 2 : 0` / `? 4 : 0`, so releasing the left button posts 256^1 = 257 (fire stays on until the next mouse event), a motion with the left button held posts no fire bit, and pressing another button while holding the left one turns fire off. | i_video.c:218, i_video.c:231, i_video.c:235-239, i_video.c:247, g_game.c:576-578 (verified; Button1Mask = 1<<8 is the X11 header's value, not opened on this host) | fire stays on after the left button is released until another mouse event; fire drops out while dragging with the left button held. | not reproduced (declared, M5b decision S4 quirk (4)); the genuine translation is compiled into the input driver as evidence | yes: `? 1 : 0` like buttons 2 and 3 | [COMPAT#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input) ("fire can stick"); M5B §2.2.4 "The Button1 bug", §2.2.7 option 3, §2.3.1 finding 1(b), §4 S4; doom-game/tests/stuckfire.rs:26-29 (comment, "quirk (4) of S4") |
| <a id="Q-i_video-253"></a>**Q-i_video-253** a MotionNotify is posted only if x != X_width/2 and y != X_height/2, but lastmousex/y were already updated, so any motion landing on either centre line is lost; with the per-tic pointer warp under -grabmouse every purely horizontal or purely vertical move is dropped. | i_video.c:253-266, i_video.c:321-333 (warp) | moving the mouse purely left-right (or purely forward-back) does nothing under -grabmouse. | not reproduced (declared, M5b decision S4: "the quirks (3) and (4) of the X layer are not reproduced") | yes: post regardless of the centre test | [COMPAT#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input) ("purely horizontal moves are dropped"); M5B §1.1.7 fact 30 and 32, §2.2.4, §2.3.1 finding 1(a), §4 S4 |

| <a id="Q-i_video-641"></a>**Q-i_video-641** the first wipe's start screen is the initial X image buffer (zero only for a fresh shared-memory segment, otherwise another user's stale segment or indeterminate malloc bytes), and the palette during the melt is undefined. | i_video.c:641-646, i_video.c:794-795 (a fresh AllocAll colormap), i_video.c:899-913, st_stuff.c:1045-1050 (the palette is uploaded only when it changes) | undefined colours and contents at the start of the first melt. | not yet ported (the screen wipe's picture is listed under [COMPAT#not-in-m5b-l…](COMPATIBILITY.md#not-in-m5b-later-milestones)); M5C §1.4.7 says a port would declare a zeroed start buffer (R8) and PLAYPAL 0 (S13); the wipe's 320 M_Random draws are reproduced (decision T6, M5c) | yes: clear the buffer, set the palette first | M5C §1.4.4 item 8, §1.4.7 (b), §1.4.8 item 7; [COMPAT#not-in-m5b-l…](COMPATIBILITY.md#not-in-m5b-later-milestones); [COMPAT#the-level-st…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-level-start-wipe-decision-t6) |
| <a id="Q-i_video-799"></a>**Q-i_video-799** the window selects no FocusChangeMask and I_GetEvent handles no focus events, so a key or button released while the window is unfocused is never seen and stays down in gamekeydown until the next level load. | i_video.c:799-802, i_video.c:201-279, g_game.c:489-496 (verified for i_video.c; g_game.c lines are the note's) | after switching away, movement or fire keeps going (observed `stuck_key_no_release`: forward 25 and fire every tic). | not reproduced (declared, M5b decision S6): the app pauses on the library's minimise/focus event and "releases every held key and button" ([COMPAT#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input) "Pause and focus", [COMPAT#platform-lim…](COMPATIBILITY.md#platform-limits) "Focus loss") | yes: handle FocusOut by releasing keys | M5B §1.1.7 fact 34, §2.2.7, §2.7, §4 S2 ruling and S6; [COMPAT#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input); [COMPAT#platform-lim…](COMPATIBILITY.md#platform-limits) |

Notes on the i_video.c rows:

- **Q-i_video-102**: M5B fact 26 marks the keysym values as external knowledge and "whether a layout produces them at index 0" as inference.
- **Q-i_video-203**: M5B labels the X behaviour an inference; "No real X server was run" (fake server model).
- **Q-i_video-218**: The Windows stuck-fire report in COMPAT is a separate matter of the app, not this quirk.
- **Q-i_video-799**: Neither is about the C, which matches the quirk as stated.
- **Q-i_video-799-2**: COMPAT names it as one of four reasons the mouse layer "is not playable"; the sweep's verification of that aggregate also found grabMouse set only by `-grabmouse` (i_video.c:83, 738) and the warp only then (321).

| <a id="Q-m_fixed-62"></a>**Q-m_fixed-62** FixedDiv returns MININT/MAXINT whenever `(abs(a)>>14) >= abs(b)`, a deliberately conservative guard that saturates quotients from about 2^30 upward that would still fit in 32 bits, instead of dividing. | m_fixed.c:62-63 | not stated. | reproduced (M2): "The guard is deliberately conservative; do not replace it with a generic saturation check"; the saturation "is tested, not replaced" (archive) | no: it is the engine's arithmetic; results would change | [M2#confirmed-ob…](M2_REFERENCE.md#confirmed-observations); [M3F2#source-and-r…](M3F2_REFERENCE.md#source-and-rust-inventory) ("FixedDiv has its original magnitude guard"); docs/validation/archive/pre-m3d3j-compatibility.md#findings-from-the-preserved-source and #implemented-m2-behavior-and-evidence-limits |
| <a id="Q-m_fixed-62-2"></a>**Q-m_fixed-62-2** FixedDiv's guard computes abs(a) and abs(b), undefined for INT_MIN; a near-parallel sight intercept can saturate to INT_MIN through an earlier FixedDiv, and the next clipping division then calls FixedDiv(x, INT_MIN), so a long sight line with that intercept has no defined original result. | m_fixed.c:62, p_sight.c:125, p_sight.c:231, p_sight.c:238 (FixedDiv with the intercept `frac` as divisor) | not stated (reachable only in extreme geometry). | refused (profile 19 / M3f2): "Profile19 returns a recoverable arithmetic error before that division, preserving canonical state"; "an intentionally excluded C failure"; historical profiles keep their own Rust numeric policy | yes: guard INT_MIN or compare in 64 bits | [COMPAT#map-extent-p…](COMPATIBILITY.md#map-extent-profile); [M3F2#quantized-in…](M3F2_REFERENCE.md#quantized-intercepts-and-the-clipping-divisor); [VAL-M3F2](validation/M3F2.md); [ARCHITECTURE](ARCHITECTURE.md) (about line 184-186); [M3F5](M3F5_REFERENCE.md) (about line 53); tools/reference/README.md:118-123; tools/reference/m3f2_reference.py:140-150, 173, 191; tools/reference/generate.py:43, 168; doom-sim/src/map_extent.rs:335-336; doom-sim/src/sight.rs:480-484; doom-sim/tests/m3f2_policies.rs:528-555 |
| <a id="Q-m_fixed-62-3"></a>**Q-m_fixed-62-3** FixedDiv(0,0) takes the guard (0 >= 0) and returns MAXINT instead of failing, so R_PointToDist with the viewer exactly on a seg's start vertex reads tantoangle[MAXINT>>DBITS], outside the table. | m_fixed.c:62-63, r_main.c:412 | not stated (a read outside the table when the viewer stands exactly on a seg's start vertex). | refused in the renderer (`RenderRefusal::TableRead`, M5a decision R12); doom-types `FixedDiv` saturates "including 0/0 -> MAX" | yes: test for a zero divisor first | [COMPAT#software-ren…](COMPATIBILITY.md#software-renderer-m5a) ("Vanilla limits (R3)": `FixedDiv`, `TableRead`); [M5A](M5A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) §1.11 hazard 2 and the R12 list ("tantoangle[FixedDiv(0,0)>>DBITS]"); [M3F2](M3F2_REFERENCE.md) (about line 120); doom-types/src/fixed.rs:15, 54-55 (comments); doom-render/src/lib.rs:136-138; doom-render/src/r_main.rs:66-67, 512-516, 567-585 (tests fixed_div_wraps_like_the_strict_build, point_to_dist_on_the_point_is_a_table_read_refusal, fixed_div_overflow_follows_the_signs_of_the_operands) |
| <a id="Q-m_fixed-69"></a>**Q-m_fixed-69** the active FixedDiv2 divides in IEEE double (`(double)a / (double)b * FRACUNIT`; the integer version is under `#if 0`), so its results depend on the floating-point unit (SSE binary64 against an i386 x87 build), and its out-of-range error says "FixedDiv: divide by zero" (unreachable through FixedDiv, which saturates first; FixedDiv2 has no other caller). | m_fixed.c:69-87 (verified; grep finds FixedDiv2 called only from m_fixed.c:64) | not stated. | reproduced with Rust's binary64 division; "FixedDiv precision (binary64 SSE versus an i386 x87 original build): open" (M5a §7 item 11); the reference build is SSE x86-64 | yes: the `#if 0` integer version | [M5A](M5A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) (summary, "the double division in FixedDiv2 (m_fixed.c:69-87)"; §4.4 tables; §7 item 11); [M3F2#source-and-r…](M3F2_REFERENCE.md#source-and-rust-inventory) ("active binary64 FixedDiv2"); docs/validation/archive/pre-m3d3j-compatibility.md#findings-from-the-preserved-source |

Notes on the m_fixed.c rows:

- **Q-m_fixed-62-2**: M5A R11 also lists `abs(INT_MIN)` among the renderer's sanitizer adapters.
- **Q-m_fixed-62-3**: the doom-types comments cite the FixedDiv2 I_Error at m_fixed.c:83-84; it is at 84-85.
- **Q-m_fixed-69**: crates/doom-types/src/fixed.rs comments cite the I_Error at m_fixed.c:83-84; it is at 84-85.

Notes on the p_doors.c rows:

- **Q-p_doors-142**: the sweep's citation 159-162 is the default branch inside the `crushed` case (151-165).
- **Q-p_doors-413**: The source comment at 364 ("only front sides can be used") sits beside `side = 0` while the sector read uses side^1, the back side.
- **Q-p_doors-416**: COMPAT's wire-23 row names DR lines "1, 26-28"; the C switch also includes 117 (blazing raise), which is not in Episode 1.
- **Q-p_doors-416**: The same switch's "JDC: bad guys never close doors" (p_doors.c:430-431) is design, not part of this quirk.
- **Q-p_doors-416-2**: the D1 line's special is cleared (p_doors.c:482) only on this path, which always creates a thinker.

| <a id="Q-p_enemy-310"></a>**Q-p_enemy-310** when a monster's P_TryMove fails with any line in spechit, P_Move sets movedir = DI_NODIR before trying P_UseSpecialLine on every entry, so even a line no monster can use (a scroller, a lift line) resets its steering; a monster's DR use always returns true, so pushing against a door "succeeds" every time and P_TryWalk draws movecount each time. | p_enemy.c:310-324, p_enemy.c:349-357 | monsters keep pushing at doors; a monster bumping a non-door special line changes direction. | reproduced (wire 19 / M3h8 step 11: "any special line in spechit resets movedir even when the use returns false"); at M3h7 Stage A only one-sided scrollers were admitted because of it | yes: reset only after a successful use | [M3H7#25-line-trig…](M3H7_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#25-line-triggers-crossing-use-shooting), §4.2; [M3H8#22-line-trig…](M3H8_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#22-line-triggers-spechit-crossing-and-monster-door-use), §2.9 table, (about line 1388); [ARCHITECTURE](ARCHITECTURE.md) (about line 1058); doom-sim/src/pursuit.rs:217-227; doom-sim/src/use_lines_tests.rs:260-268; commits 4a5cd97 "sim: monsters use special lines when a move fails", bfbc2b3 "sim: give between-tic operations the runtime line specials under movers" |

| <a id="Q-p_enemy-408"></a>**Q-p_enemy-408** `if (P_Random() > 200 \|\| abs(deltay)>abs(deltax))` always draws, because the draw is the left operand; the second condition never saves the draw; and the old direction is retried even when it equals a direction that already failed. | p_enemy.c:408-414, p_enemy.c:441-446 | not stated (RNG stream). | reproduced (M3d3b, "unconditional axis-priority draw") | no: fixes RNG order | [M3D3B#new-directio…](M3D3B_REFERENCE.md#new-direction-search); docs/validation/archive/pre-m3d3j-m3d3b_reference.md#new-direction-search, pre-m3d3j-compatibility.md#m3d3b-pursuit-profile-dsim-v9 |
| <a id="Q-p_enemy-512"></a>**Q-p_enemy-512** the `c++ == 2 \|\| lastlook == stop` test comes before the health and sight tests, so at most two active-player encounters are examined (repeats of one player count), and the stop slot can end the scan before that slot is examined. | p_enemy.c:512-534 | a monster may fail to notice a visible player (in a 3-4 player game; from the C, also on the first call in single player when lastlook starts one past the player's slot). | reproduced (M3d3a, 20 scan scenarios including "candidate/stop limits") | yes: test the count after health/sight | [M3D3A#player-scan-…](M3D3A_REFERENCE.md#player-scan-and-lastlook); docs/validation/archive/pre-m3d3j-m3d3a_reference.md#player-scan-and-lastlook, pre-m3d3j-compatibility.md#m3d3a-perception-profile-dsim-v8 |

Notes on the p_enemy.c rows:

- **Q-p_enemy-115**: the sweep marked it "design"; it is kept because the documents single it out and a reader expecting one line to block would be surprised.
- **Q-p_enemy-174**: The documents cite 174-190 or 174-192; the function is 174-192.
- **Q-p_enemy-310**: M3H8 §2.2 explains that the spechit entries are those of the failed P_CheckPosition (none when a thing blocked, all when the fit test failed), which is how a closed DR door's line gets in.
- **Q-p_enemy-512**: the single-player case in "player" is the register builder's reading of the C (stop = lastlook-1), not a document's statement.
- **Q-p_enemy-693**: From the C, `actor->angle &= (7<<29)` at 695 has the same signed-shift form (the register builder's reading).
- **Q-p_enemy-725**: two E entries contributed (selection sound; A_SargAttack has no sound).
- **Q-p_enemy-738**: The consequence "a Baron that can never move keeps movecount nonzero and never fires" is stated only in tools/reference/m4b_wad.py:362-365 (searched docs/*.md and docs/validation/*.md for "never fires", "can never move", "keeps movecount": no match); the gate itself is in M3D3C and M4B.
- **Q-p_enemy-802**: From the C, the chaingunner's attack plays sfx_shotgn (855), not a chaingun sound (the register builder's reading; compare COMPAT T13 row 6).
- **Q-p_enemy-865**: M3D3E notes that the real table has no value 40 (39 and 41 occur).
- **Q-p_enemy-1613**: The Rust comments cite 1729-1733 / 1729-1734; the E1 branch is 1731-1734 inside the `switch(gameepisode)` at 1729.

| <a id="Q-p_floor-68"></a>**Q-p_floor-68** because a turbo "lower" can raise a floor in one step, a repeatable 70 SR or 98 WR line tagging two adjacent sectors raises both by 8 on every use, with nothing in C bounding the height until `fixed_t` overflows. | p_floor.c:307-315, p_floor.c:66-79 (verified); p_switch.c:592-596 and p_spec.c:909-912 (verified: the repeatable 70 and 98 dispatch); p_switch.c:592-596; p_floor.c:307-315; p_spec.c:297-316 | each press of a "lower floor" switch raises the floors another step (M4A example 992/1,008 to 1,032 in three presses) | refused (`MapScaleError::Arithmetic` past +-1,024, wire 23 / M4a, independent review A1); wires 19-22 admit only the single-use W1 36 and prove heights within +-512 at load (`MoverHeights`) | yes: clamp the destination to the floor | [M4A#14-correctio…](M4A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#14-corrections-and-findings-from-phase-2) ("Runtime sector heights are unbounded under wire 23"); [COMPATIBILITY#runtime-witn…](COMPATIBILITY.md#runtime-witnesses) (`Arithmetic` row: "a repeatable turbo floor or door can ratchet heights past any bound"); [VAL-M4A](validation/M4A.md) (review A1); [COMPATIBILITY#runtime-witn…](COMPATIBILITY.md#runtime-witnesses) (line 2446); [M4A](M4A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) §14 (independent review A1, line 1938); doom-sim/src/player_episode1_movers_tests.rs:1467-1534, 1348-1354, 930-936 |

| <a id="Q-p_inter-817"></a>**Q-p_inter-817** the thrust `damage*(FRACUNIT>>3)*100/mass` is computed in `int`, which overflows for damage of 2,622 or more, so the telefrag's 10,000 gives 8,192,000,000, which wraps to -397,934,592 before the division (signed overflow, undefined in C). | p_inter.c:817; p_map.c:105 (`P_DamageMobj (thing, tmthing, tmthing, 10000)`) | a telefragged body is thrown by a wrapped, sign-flipped amount (clamped per axis to MAXMOVE) | reproduced (wire 24 / M4b, decision I3: "the wrapped 32-bit value that the genuine strict builds compute (-397,934,592/mass)", observed in TFRAG1); at M4a it was listed as needing a declared wrapping adapter | yes: widen or clamp the product | [COMPATIBILITY#original-epi…](COMPATIBILITY.md#original-episode-1-part-2-teleporters-the-baron-of-hell-a_bossdeath-and-hard-skill-wire-24) (Stomping); [M4A#336-draws-in…](M4A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#336-draws-in-order-for-one-successful-teleport), §3.13, §13; [M4B](M4B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) (T8.5); doom-sim/src/combat.rs:213-216, 571-577, 658-668, src/original_player.rs:715, src/diagnostic.rs:2012-2014, src/teleport_tests.rs:108-117, 382-388 (lines from the sweep); tools/reference/m4b_build.py:1-17, 42-52, tools/reference/README.md:742-746 (lines from the sweep); commit 81d2f84 |

| <a id="Q-p_inter-904"></a>**Q-p_inter-904** the retarget block sets a wounded barrel's `target` to its first non-lethal wounder with threshold 100, and a barrel never runs `A_Chase` to lower the threshold, so that first wounder stays the barrel's `target` and becomes the source of its explosion (`A_Explode` passes `target`); a barrel killed by its first hit has no source. | p_inter.c:904-915; p_enemy.c:1598-1601 | kill credit, frags and monster retargets from a barrel blast go to the first wounder, not the killer; a monster that wounded the barrel can be hurt by "its own" blast | reproduced (wire 21 / M3h9b: "the first-wounder bomb source") | unknown | [COMPATIBILITY#original-mon…](COMPATIBILITY.md#original-monsters-with-exploding-barrels-pickup-scale-and-every-thing-angle); [M3H9#28-barrels-m…](M3H9_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#28-barrels-mt_barrel-2035-and-the-explosion-chain); [M3H9B#23-p_damagem…](M3H9B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) (item 7) |

| <a id="Q-p_inter-914"></a>**Q-p_inter-914** a monster hit by `P_RadiusAttack` in its spawn state enters its see state inside `P_DamageMobj`, which runs `A_Chase` at once; if that move relinks it, the block walk (which reads `bnext` after each callback, newest-linked first) revisits or skips things. | p_inter.c:904-915; p_mobj.c:77-78 (action on state entry); p_maputl.c:529-535 (P_BlockThingsIterator reads `bnext` after the callback); p_map.c:1230-1232 | one barrel blast can damage the same monster twice ("another Imp is damaged twice (39 + 39) and dies") | reproduced (wire 21 / M3h9b, C scenario `monster_wakes_mid_walk`) | yes: snapshot the candidates first | [COMPATIBILITY#original-mon…](COMPATIBILITY.md#original-monsters-with-exploding-barrels-pickup-scale-and-every-thing-angle) ("re-reading the next link after every victim"); [M3H9B#23-p_damagem…](M3H9B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) (item 7), §5.2; [VAL-M3H9B#original-c-e…](validation/M3H9B.md#original-c-evidence) |

Notes on the p_inter.c rows:

- **Q-p_inter-692**: COMPATIBILITY (wire 18) and M3H7 say the null-source branch is unreachable there because only players read damage floors; monster-on-monster kills reach it.
- **Q-p_inter-705**: the barrel, which has no `A_Fall`, is the next row.
- **Q-p_inter-726**: the strict xdeath threshold (p_inter.c:719) in the same entries is dropped as a boundary choice (see Dropped).
- **Q-p_inter-817**: Fixing it does change behaviour; it is a small change.
- **Q-p_inter-820**: The comment shows it is intended.
- **Q-p_inter-838**: M4A §3.2.3 was written before the port ("The sector-11 cap is also absent"); COMPATIBILITY records it as admitted under wire 23.
- **Q-p_inter-894**: It is a small change that alters RNG order.

Notes on the p_map.c rows:

- **Q-p_map-191**: M4B facts 6-9 are "kept as written" and superseded by the correction paragraph (finding 29): the earlier text assumed the M3h5 step bound (2r - 2 = 10 units) held for this ball, but 15 > 10.
- **Q-p_map-191**: COMPATIBILITY and ROADMAP cite the same lines (191-195, 374-442).
- **Q-p_map-214**: M3H8 §2.9 ("blocking flags would keep it out for non-missiles only") agrees with the C.
- **Q-p_map-240**: M3H7 §4.2 assumed scrollers are "usually one-sided"; M3H8 §1 measured that E1M1's are two-sided.
- **Q-p_map-240**: The use-trace effect of the same line is the p_map.c:1113 row.
- **Q-p_map-956**: the sky hack itself is deliberate; the row is the front-sector-only reading.
- **Q-p_map-956**: M3D1 already notes "sky suppression is another branch".
- **Q-p_map-1113**: All agree the trace stops, which is what the C does (1122 returns false regardless).
- **Q-p_map-1227**: COMPAT separately lists "the first-wounder bomb source" among wire 21's reproduced quirks.
- **Q-p_map-1269**: the gib rule itself is generic for every corpse (wire 19: "a corpse that does not fit becomes giblets"); the row is the dying-barrel consequence.
- **Q-p_map-1332**: the block box's MAXRADIUS extension is made at load (P_GroupLines), not in P_ChangeSector.

through P_BoxOnLineSide's two-corner tests a box whose corner lies exactly on a diagonal wall is blocked from the front but not from the back, a box approaching from the back can stop up to `1/L` units past the line, and a player running straight into a convex 45°/45° tip slides in place. | p_maputl.c:91-99 (products at 94-95, `if (right < left) return 0;` at 97), p_maputl.c:139-152 (P_BoxOnLineSide corner tests for ST_POSITIVE/ST_NEGATIVE, `if (p1 == p2)` at 150); | <a id="Q-p_maputl-428"></a>**Q-p_maputl-428** things are linked into the BLOCKMAP cell of their centre only, and P_PathTraverse offers a trace the things of the cells it visits without the MAXRADIUS widening that P_CheckPosition uses, so a hitscan or autoaim trace that crosses a thing's box in a neighbouring cell never sees it. | p_maputl.c:428-442 (link by centre at head), p_maputl.c:854-857 (things offered per visited cell); compare p_map.c:415-423 (P_CheckPosition widens by MAXRADIUS "because mobj_ts are grouped into mapblocks based on their origin point") | a shot can miss a thing that overlaps the aim line when its centre lies in a neighbouring block row (a pistol trace at `y = −0.007` never sees barrels at `y = 0`); autoaim can miss a target the shot still hits | reproduced (M3d1 trace bodies: "center-cell thing heads"; observed in the genuine engine in the M4a synthetic runs, "a hitscan hits a thing only if the trace enters the BLOCKMAP cell holding the thing's centre"; M3h9b: "Not a quirk of the harness but of `P_PathTraverse`; it matters for matching E1M1 hitscans") | yes: widen the visited cells by MAXRADIUS | [M4A#14-correctio…](M4A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#14-corrections-and-findings-from-phase-2) ("Engine facts observed in the synthetic runs", line 1929; barrel packing, line 1899); [M3H9B#13-correctio…](M3H9B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#13-corrections-and-findings-from-phase-2-kept-with-the-original-text-above) (§2.8 bullet, lines 1073-1076); [M3D1#original-sou…](M3D1_REFERENCE.md#original-source-findings); [M3H4#61-c-harness](M3H4_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#61-c-harness) (lines 442-448, "Things are linked only in the block of their center") |

Notes on the p_maputl.c rows:

- **Q-p_maputl-428**: M3H9B ties its pistol example to the start nudge as well (p_maputl.c:777 row); the y = −0.007 start is not on a boundary, so the miss there comes from the centre-cell rule.
- **Q-p_maputl-472**: COMPATIBILITY's diagonal policy notes that "real node builders list a line only in the cells it crosses, which real-map loading must revisit".

| <a id="Q-p_mobj-152"></a>**Q-p_mobj-152** P_XYMovement splits a step only when xmove or ymove is greater than MAXMOVE/2, never when one is below -MAXMOVE/2, so a fast negative move goes in one step while the same positive move goes in two; the step uses `/2` (toward zero) and the remainder `>>= 1` (floor), so odd negative values split unevenly. | p_mobj.c:150-164 (verified; test at 152, `xmove/2` at 154-155, `>>= 1` at 156-157); MAXMOVE p_local.h:54 | not stated directly; the consequences are the barrel corner jump and the teleport continuation below (rockets split only for headings near +x/+y, M4a 3.5.3) | reproduced (M2 vectors; M3a "positive-only ... splitting"; wire 14 map-scale policy; wire 15 "source positive-only subdivision"; wire 21 "Quirks reproduced ... the positive-only halving of `P_XYMovement` steps"; M4a rockets) | yes: compare absolute values, halve symmetrically | [M2#confirmed-ob…](M2_REFERENCE.md#confirmed-observations) ("This negative/positive and odd-negative asymmetry must remain"); [M3A#source-behav…](M3A_REFERENCE.md#source-behavior); [M3B](M3B_REFERENCE.md); [M3H3#2-original-c…](M3H3_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#2-original-c-facts); [M3H9B](M3H9B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md):293; [M4A#353-rocket-l…](M4A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#353-rocket-launcher-pickup-2003-mt_misc27-and-mt_rocket) (also 3.3.3, 3.10.4); [COMPATIBILITY#original-pla…](COMPATIBILITY.md#original-player-grounded-knockback) (line 764) and #original-monsters-with-exploding-barrels-pickup-scale-and-every-thing-angle (line 2243); doom-sim/src/barrels.rs:150-163; doom-sim/src/player_barrel_combat_tests.rs:1184-1187; archive pre-m3d3j-m3d2b_reference.md#xyz-and-collision-ordering and pre-m3d3j-compatibility.md (M2 XY paragraph) |
| <a id="Q-p_mobj-152-2"></a>**Q-p_mobj-152-2** Because a negative move of up to 30 units is not halved and P_CheckPosition tests only the destination box, a barrel (radius 10) with more than 20 units of momentum per axis can clear a wall corner in one P_TryMove and put its centre outside the room. | p_mobj.c:150-164 (verified); p_map.c:375-442 (P_CheckPosition, box built at the destination 394-397, verified), p_map.c:451 (P_TryMove, verified) | a barrel hit hard enough (an API push, or the fall-forward thrust of a lethal hit from far below) can fly out of the map through a corner | reproduced up to the escape, then refused: "the Rust move does the same, and then ... refuses the tic with `MapScaleError::Escape` when the barrel's centre has left the room (state preserved)" (wire 21 / M3h9b); the player's own corner jump is the separate wire-16 witness | yes: always split, or sweep the move | [M3H9B#13-correctio…](M3H9B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#13-corrections-and-findings-from-phase-2-kept-with-the-original-text-above) (F3 bullet); [COMPATIBILITY#original-mon…](COMPATIBILITY.md#original-monsters-with-exploding-barrels-pickup-scale-and-every-thing-angle) (lines 2213-2222); doom-sim/src/barrels.rs:150-163; test `a_barrel_jumping_the_wall_corner_refuses_the_tic_with_escape` (doom-sim/src/player_barrel_combat_tests.rs:1184-1187) |

| <a id="Q-p_mobj-794"></a>**Q-p_mobj-794** `ANG45 * (mthing->angle/45)` multiplies an int constant (0x20000000) by an int quotient of a short angle, so every quotient outside -4..3 (THING angle 180 and above, or -225 and below) overflows signed int; only the original build's two's-complement wrap gives the intended angle. | p_mobj.c:794 (P_SpawnMapThing), 671 (P_SpawnPlayer), 400 (P_NightmareRespawn), 627 (P_RespawnSpecials) (all verified); ANG45 tables.h:68 (verified); mapthing_t.angle short doomdata.h:207 | nothing observable in play (the wrap gives the intended octant) | refused under wires 12-20 for quotients outside -4..3 (`MapSpawnError::Angle`, M3f3 profile 20 "admits only selected Demon THING angles whose truncated quotient is in [-4, 3]"); reproduced from wire 21 ("A declared reproduced quirk of the original build", decision E9; C evidence under `-fwrapv` and an unsigned rewrite, byte-identical) | yes: make the arithmetic unsigned | [COMPATIBILITY#expanded-map…](COMPATIBILITY.md#expanded-map-demon-profile), #original-former-humans-and-multi-monster-encounters ("THING angles"), #original-monsters-with-exploding-barrels-pickup-scale-and-every-thing-angle ("Every THING angle", line 2176); [M3F3#original-sig…](M3F3_REFERENCE.md#original-signed-spawn-angle-boundary); [VAL-M3F3](validation/M3F3.md):77; [M3H2#reachability…](M3H2_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#reachability-findings-from-the-observer) (line 134); [M3H9#6-new-owner-…](M3H9_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#6-new-owner-decisions-e1e8) (E9); [M3H9B](M3H9B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) (line 371); [M3H9C](M3H9C_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md):66, 216, 438-440, 852; [M4B](M4B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md):739, 838; [M5C](M5C_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md):1302, 1318; tools/reference/m3h9b_reference.py:95; tools/reference/m3h9c_build.py:107-110; tools/reference/m3f3_reference.py:82-97; tools/iwad_counts.py:1084-1087; doom-sim/src/e1m1_rules.rs:270-273; doom-sim/src/spawning.rs:589-592, 807-813 |
| <a id="Q-p_mobj-794-2"></a>**Q-p_mobj-794-2** The map angle is divided by 45 with C truncation before scaling, so any angle that is not a multiple of 45 snaps toward zero: positive angles round down (359 gives 315 degrees) and negative ones round up (-1 gives 0, -50 gives 315 degrees). | p_mobj.c:794 (verified; the same expression at 400, 627, 671) | things face the 45-degree multiple toward zero from their map angle | reproduced (M3c "signed angle quantization"; M3d3i; wire 21 for every angle, "`ANG45 x (angle / 45)` modulo 2^32") | yes: scale exactly; changes existing maps' facings | [M3C#original-sou…](M3C_REFERENCE.md#original-source-findings); [M3D3I#spawn-initia…](M3D3I_REFERENCE.md#spawn-initialization-and-observable-order); [M3H6](M3H6_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md):87; [M3H2](M3H2_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md):134; doom-sim/src/spawning.rs:589-592, 807-813; doom-sim/src/e1m1_rules.rs:270-273; doom-sim/src/player_e1m1_tests.rs:1600-1602; doom-sim/src/player_barrels_tests.rs:439-441; doom-sim/src/player_e1m1_soak_tests.rs:757-762; archive pre-m3d3j-m3d3i_reference.md#spawn-initialization-and-observable-order, pre-m3d3j-compatibility.md#m3d3c-actor-state-profile |
| <a id="Q-p_mobj-819"></a>**Q-p_mobj-819** P_SpawnPuff and P_SpawnBlood add a random jitter to the given z and spawn there with no check against the sector's floor and ceiling, so an impact can sit outside its sector until P_ZMovement clips it. | p_mobj.c:811-831 (P_SpawnPuff, jitter 819), 838-859 (P_SpawnBlood, jitter 847) | a puff observed 30 units below the floor when a spread shot missed down a staircase (M3H4) | reproduced (M3h4: "impacts are excluded from V and clipped by `P_ZMovement` as in the source") | yes: clamp z to the span | [M3H4#26-hitscan-t…](M3H4_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#26-hitscan-through-openings), §6.1, §8 |
| <a id="Q-p_mobj-819-2"></a>**Q-p_mobj-819-2** Jitter and spread are written `(P_Random()-P_Random())<<N`, whose two calls have no specified order in C and whose difference may be negative before the left shift, so which draw is "left" depends on the compiler and the shift is formally undefined. | p_mobj.c:819, 847, 910; p_pspr.c:482, 512, 637, 720, 724 (all verified); p_enemy.c:795, 816, 839, 860 | nothing observable with the original compiler's order (another order changes puff heights and shot spread) | reproduced under a declared adapter that "explicitly evaluates left then right RNG and multiplies" (M3d1, M3d2, M3d2b, M3d3c-e; all wires: "Replaces the source's undefined negative signed left shift by multiplication"); COMPATIBILITY: "left draw first, as observed in the genuine engine" (wire 24) and "inherited ... ordered spread/jitter qualifications are not new portable ISO-C evidence" | yes: draw into two locals in order | [M3D1#original-sou…](M3D1_REFERENCE.md#original-source-findings), #c-evidence-and-exclusions; [M3D2#evidence-pla…](M3D2_REFERENCE.md#evidence-plan-and-c-policies); [M3D2B#reference-pl…](M3D2B_REFERENCE.md#reference-plan-and-c-policies); [M3D3C](M3D3C_REFERENCE.md):124; [M2](M2_REFERENCE.md):56; [WEAPON_FORMULAS](WEAPON_FORMULAS.md); [COMPATIBILITY#genuine-play…](COMPATIBILITY.md#genuine-player-pistol-combat), #maintenance-compatibility-boundary, the wire-24 Baron section; doom-sim/src/weapons.rs:712-713, 743-745; archive pre-m3d3j-m3d1_reference.md#c-evidence-and-exclusions, pre-m3d3j-m3d2_reference.md, pre-m3d3j-m3d2b_reference.md, pre-m3d3j-m3d3c_reference.md, pre-m3d3j-compatibility.md#m3d1--explicit-profile-5, pre-m3d3j-reference-tools.md |
| <a id="Q-p_mobj-876"></a>**Q-p_mobj-876** P_CheckMissileSpawn adds half the momentum to x, y and z directly, then calls P_TryMove at those coordinates; if that fails the missile explodes at the half-stepped position while still linked to its old subsector, blockmap cell and sector lists. | p_mobj.c:868-882 (verified; raw writes 876-878, `P_TryMove` and explode 880-881) | not stated (the C implies a fireball fired point-blank at a wall explodes slightly ahead of where it is linked) | reproduced (M3d2b "Preserve that distinction in canonical state"; wire 13 / M3h2 "A failed move keeps the half-stepped coordinates with the old subsector, blockmap and sector links"; wire 24 Baron "A contact during the spawn half step draws the damage, runs `P_DamageMobj` and then explodes") | yes: restore or relink on failure (changes spawn positions) | [M3D2B#spawn-and-ow…](M3D2B_REFERENCE.md#spawn-and-ownership); [M3H2#missile-life…](M3H2_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#missile-lifecycle-p_mobjc-p_mapc); [M3H3](M3H3_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md):225; [ARCHITECTURE#original-mis…](ARCHITECTURE.md#original-missiles-and-typed-action-dispatch) (line 841); [COMPATIBILITY](COMPATIBILITY.md) wire-24 Baron section (line 2572); doom-sim/src/demon_expanded_map.rs:568-569; doom-sim/src/baron_tests.rs:764-768 (test `spawn_step_hit_draws_damage_pain_and_explosion`); doom-sim/src/player_e1m1_tests.rs:1045; archive pre-m3d3j-m3d2b_reference.md#spawn-and-ownership, pre-m3d3j-compatibility.md#m3d2b-projectile-profile-dsim-v7 |
| <a id="Q-p_mobj-900"></a>**Q-p_mobj-900** P_SpawnMissile spawns the missile 32 units above the shooter but computes momz from the target's z minus the shooter's z, so the trajectory runs parallel to the feet-to-feet line and ends 32 units above the target's z; the travel time is an integer division clamped to at least 1. | p_mobj.c:898-900, 917-923 | not stated | reproduced (M3d2b, 41 scenarios: "Spawn height +32 is deliberately absent from that vertical difference") | yes: include the offset (changes trajectories) | [M3D2B#spawn-and-ow…](M3D2B_REFERENCE.md#spawn-and-ownership); archive pre-m3d3j-m3d2b_reference.md#spawn-and-ownership |

Notes on the p_mobj.c rows:

- **Q-p_mobj-60**: the document names only the function; the lines are the register builder's.
- **Q-p_mobj-122**: the document names only the function.
- **Q-p_mobj-152-2**: M3H9B §6 F3 proved the step bound for the player and monsters; §13 shows it fails for a barrel (radius 10); the note found it with fuzz seeds 1123, 1184, 1367, 1428, 1489, 1550.
- **Q-p_mobj-176**: M3H7 adds that the removal skips the P_ExplodeMissile draw and that P_XYMovement reads the back sector of ceilingline whatever side the missile is on.
- **Q-p_mobj-176**: The hitscan sky returns of PTR_ShootTraverse (p_map.c:956, 963, verified as sky tests) belong to the p_map.c rows.
- **Q-p_mobj-322**: the document names only the function.
- **Q-p_mobj-331**: No document was found discussing that comment.
- **Q-p_mobj-428**: the document names only the function.
- **Q-p_mobj-566**: a hazard named by the note, not an observed behaviour.
- **Q-p_mobj-819-2**: The p_enemy.c sites belong to that file's rows too.

| <a id="Q-p_plats-173"></a>**Q-p_plats-173** EV_DoPlat takes the plat_t from Z_Malloc and never clears it, so fields such as `count` (and `oldstatus`, and `low` for one type, next row) hold whatever the zone block held until first written. | p_plats.c:173-181 (verified; allocation at 173, fields written 176-181 and per type 185-249); count first written at p_plats.c:75, 84, 113 and read only in the waiting state at 120 | nothing observable in play ("no admitted path reads an uninitialised field before writing it", M3H8 N6) | reproduced as zero under a declared adapter (decision N6, M3h8: the C harness zero-fills mover allocations and DSIM35 starts these fields at 0; the whole-engine driver's `ZeroFill`, M3h9c / wire 22 onward) | yes: clear the record after Z_Malloc | [M3H8](M3H8_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) (line 896; N6 at 1137-1145, 1176, 1309); [VAL-M3H9C](validation/M3H9C.md):128-133; [M4A](M4A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md); [M5A](M5A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md); [M5B](M5B_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md); tools/reference/m3h9c_build.py:134; tools/reference/m3h9c_driver.c:59-72 (also m4a_driver.c:79-92, m5a_driver.c:107-120, m5b_driver.c:171-184, m5c_driver.c:213-226); tools/reference/level_snapshot.py:39-40, 925-940; tools/reference/README.md:631-635; doom-sim/src/movers.rs:9-11, 74, 99-101, 153; commits 883ffd4, 07d200d |
| <a id="Q-p_plats-185"></a>**Q-p_plats-185** EV_DoPlat's raiseToNearestAndChange case writes speed, high, wait and status but never `low`, so if the plat is blocked while rising it reverses toward an uninitialised `low`, and with wait 0 its counter runs below zero. | p_plats.c:185-195 (verified; `low` set only at 209, 222, 235 for the other types); p_plats.c:73-79 (blocked rise turns down), 108-117, 119-127 (`!--plat->count`) | a lift that reverses and sinks toward the zone's stale value (0 under the zero-fill) and does not come back (observed in the dumps: "descending to low = 0 and waiting with count running below zero") | refused (`PlatBlocked`, wire 23, decision H7; "Observed at tic 91 (PLATS2)") | yes: initialise plat->low | [M4A#317-plats-20…](M4A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#317-plats-20-s1-22-w1-62-sr) (3.1.7, 3.13, H7 at line 1728, section 14); [COMPATIBILITY#runtime-witn…](COMPATIBILITY.md#runtime-witnesses) (line 2444, wire 23); doom-sim/src/movers.rs:1108-1113 |
| <a id="Q-p_plats-214"></a>**Q-p_plats-214** A down-wait-up lift takes `high` from the floor at activation, and T_MovePlane's last up step returns pastdest with the floor left short when the move is blocked, so the next activation uses that lower floor as `high` and the lift ratchets down by less than one step per cycle, never up. | p_plats.c:207-218 (`high = sec->floorheight` at 214, verified); p_floor.c:97-108 (blocked final step restores lastpos and still returns pastdest, verified); p_plats.c:82-88 | not stated beyond "the floor can ratchet down by less than one step per cycle" | not determined from the documents (M3H8 §2.5 describes it and its height fixpoint covers it; no reproduction or refusal is stated) | yes: keep the original high | [M3H8#25-the-lift-…](M3H8_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#25-the-lift-88-and-activeplats) (lines 220-225, 574) |
| <a id="Q-p_plats-298"></a>**Q-p_plats-298** P_AddActivePlat calls I_Error when all 30 activeplats slots are full, and P_StartButton calls I_Error when all 16 button slots are busy. | p_plats.c:288-299 (verified; I_Error at 298); MAXPLATS 30 p_spec.h:306, MAXBUTTONS 16 p_spec.h:244 (verified); p_switch.c:189 | the game exits with "P_AddActivePlat: no more plats!" or "P_StartButton: no button slots left!" | refused at load (`MapScaleError::MoverCount`, wire 19 onward: "30 load, 31 are refused") | yes: grow the tables or recycle slots | [COMPATIBILITY#original-mon…](COMPATIBILITY.md#original-monsters-with-doors-lifts-floors-switches-and-the-exit) (line 1797); [M3H7#27-doors-lif…](M3H7_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#27-doors-lifts-and-floors) (line 835); [M3H8](M3H8_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md); doom-sim/src/player_episode1_movers_tests.rs:1434-1441, 1054-1059 |
