| <a id="Q-p_pspr-151"></a>**Q-p_pspr-151** P_BringUpWeapon sets the weapon's y to WEAPONBOTTOM (128) and enters the up state, whose A_Raise runs at once and subtracts 6, so the first displayed height is 122; A_Raise can also reach the ready state within the same call chain. | p_pspr.c:138-153 (verified; `sy = WEAPONBOTTOM` at 151, P_SetPsprite at 153), p_pspr.c:422-441 (A_Raise, `sy -= RAISESPEED` at 429, verified) | the weapon is already 6 units up on its first frame | reproduced (M3d2, 38 scenarios: "initial Y is 122, not 128") | yes: start at WEAPONBOTTOM + RAISESPEED | [M3D2#psprites](M3D2_REFERENCE.md#psprites) (line 36); archive pre-m3d3j-m3d2_reference.md#psprites, pre-m3d3j-compatibility.md#m3d2--explicit-profile-6, pre-m3d3j-readme.md |
| <a id="Q-p_pspr-255"></a>**Q-p_pspr-255** P_FireWeapon enters the attack psprite state (running its first action at once) and only then calls P_NoiseAlert, so an immediate shot (chaingun) can damage and retarget a monster before the noise reaches it, while windup weapons alert before their later shot. | p_pspr.c:246-257 (verified; P_SetPsprite at 255, P_NoiseAlert at 256) | not stated (monsters wake when the trigger is pulled, and an instant shot lands before that alert) | reproduced (M3d2 "THEN calls P_NoiseAlert"; wire 8: "Fist/pistol/shotgun windups place noise before their later attack action"; the chaingun "can damage/retarget the Demon first") | yes: alert before setting the state (changes demo sync) | [M3D2#weapon-defin…](M3D2_REFERENCE.md#weapon-definitions-and-attacks) (line 69); [COMPATIBILITY#original-pla…](COMPATIBILITY.md#original-player-weapon-expansion); archive pre-m3d3j-m3d2_reference.md#weapon-definitions-and-attacks |
| <a id="Q-p_pspr-487"></a>**Q-p_pspr-487** A_Punch draws damage, then the two spread draws, runs the attack, and only then plays the punch sound and turns the player toward `linetarget`, so the facing uses the target's position after the damage and any immediate retarget or chase it caused. | p_pspr.c:467-495 (verified; damage 476, spread 482, aim 483, attack 484, sound and turn 487-494) | not stated | reproduced (M3g8: "sound and facing use the target's position after damage and any immediate retarget/chase action") | yes: record the angle before the attack | [M3G8](M3G8_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md) (A_Punch row, line 14) |
| <a id="Q-p_pspr-515"></a>**Q-p_pspr-515** A_Saw aims and attacks with `MELEERANGE+1` ("so the puff doesn't skip the flash"), one raw fixed-point unit more than the fist, purely so that P_SpawnPuff's `attackrange == MELEERANGE` test fails and a wall hit spawns a normal puff instead of S_PUFF3. | p_pspr.c:514-516 (verified; comment at 514 with the typo "se"), p_mobj.c:828-830 | sawing a wall shows a normal puff with its spark frames | reproduced (M4a part 1 / wire 23; "the trace endpoint still uses `range >> 16` = 64 units, and the puff is never forced to `S_PUFF3`") | no: deliberate, as the comment says | [M4A#352-chainsaw…](M4A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md#352-chainsaw-pickup-2005-mt_misc26) (lines 913-915) and section 14 (line 1926); doom-sim/src/player_pistol.rs:14-15, 682-691; doom-sim/src/player_episode1_weapons_tests.rs:96-99 |

Notes on the p_spec.c rows:

- **Q-p_spec-297**: The first Rust code ignored the -500 start (independent review finding 2, M3h8).
- **Q-p_spec-440**: The teleport's own missing guard is a separate row (p_telept.c:74).
- **Q-p_spec-503**: From the C (not stated in any merged entry): the type filter lists only MT_ROCKET, MT_PLASMA, MT_BFG, MT_TROOPSHOT, MT_HEADSHOT and MT_BRUISERSHOT, so other missile types are not filtered for 4, 10 and 88.
- **Q-p_spec-543**: the teleport case, where a back-side crossing also spends the line, is its own row (p_spec.c:658).
- **Q-p_spec-1065**: light and door sector specials are cleared at spawn by their own spawn functions, so the I_Error is reached only by numbers no spawn function handles (from the C, not stated in the entry).
- **Q-p_spec-1151**: Slot 15 is its own row.
- **Q-p_spec-1151-2**: entries citing the slot-15 overrun: A `switch-release-slot-15-reads-past-buttonlist` (the only entry for this alone), and the slot-15 parts of B, G1a, G1b, G2, H1b, H2.

### p_switch.c

Notes on the r_data.c rows:

- **Q-r_data-541**: A's status line ("refused / ported per finding 41") concerns the flat lookup, not patchlookup.
- **Q-r_data-642**: M5A hazard 15 also names r_data.c:84-93 (the on-disk maptexture_t with a pointer field).
- **Q-r_data-642**: A portability fact of the Linux source rather than gameplay.
- **Q-r_data-701**: texture number 0 is also the first real texture; vanilla shares that number with "-" (m3h8_geometry.py).

| <a id="Q-r_draw-142-2"></a>**Q-r_draw-142-2** Because the column drawers wrap the texel index at 128 whatever the texture height, a wall texture taller than 128 rows shows its first 128 rows again below them and its rows 128 and up are never drawn. | r_draw.c:142, r_draw.c:247 | a tall texture tiles every 128 rows instead of showing its lower part (evident from the C) | reproduced (the drawers are ported with the same `& 127`, COMPAT "Undefined texels (R4)": "Wall columns index `& 127` whatever the texture height", M5a) | yes: wrap by the texture height | M5A §2.2.2, §2.10 hazard 6; [COMPAT#software-ren…](COMPATIBILITY.md#software-renderer-m5a) (R4); doom-render/src/r_data.rs:13 (comment) |

| <a id="Q-r_plane-398"></a>**Q-r_plane-398** R_DrawPlanes always draws the sky with colormap 0, ignoring sector light, extralight and fixedcolormap ("Because of this hack, sky is not affected by INVUL inverse mapping"). | r_plane.c:396-405 (404 `dc_colormap = colormaps;`) | the sky stays its normal colours under invulnerability (and under light amplification) | reproduced as far as admitted: frames with light amplification (fixed colour map 1) are covered (COMPAT, M5a); invulnerability is not admitted by the simulation, so the inverted-world-normal-sky case is not ported (no milestone named) | yes: use fixedcolormap for the sky | M5A §1.4, §1.11 hazard 11; [COMPAT#software-ren…](COMPATIBILITY.md#software-renderer-m5a) ("Invulnerability" row) |

- **Q-r_plane-438**: defined only by struct layout; undefined in ISO C as written.

| <a id="Q-r_segs-81"></a>**Q-r_segs-81** A two-sided wall whose top or bottom texture is chosen on the unshifted heights but whose `>>4` shifted-height test fails draws with the pixhigh/pixlow (and steps) left by the previous such wall, in the same frame or an earlier one, because they are file-scope globals that nothing resets. | r_segs.c:81-84, 570-573, 589-592, 693-704 | not stated (a top or bottom wall edge drawn with another wall's step) | reproduced ("the four values persist in the render state (`WallSteps`)", DRST version 2, M5A item 42, M5a); COMPAT: "A `pixhigh` or `pixlow` left over from an earlier wall is reproduced, not refused" | yes: initialise them per seg | M5A §1.3 step 12, §1.11 hazard 8, items 36 and 42; [COMPAT#software-ren…](COMPATIBILITY.md#software-renderer-m5a) ("Vanilla limits (R3)" paragraph); VAL-M5A (Rust tests, "the carried `pixhigh`/`pixlow`"); doom-render/src/lib.rs:141-142; doom-render/src/r_segs.rs:119-132, 501-506; doom-render/src/state.rs:21-25; doom-render/src/r_bsp.rs:748-752 (test equal_floors_and_ceilings_pick_no_lower_or_upper_texture_and_leave_the_steps_alone); commit 8b5c4be ("persistent pixhigh/pixlow in the render state") |
| <a id="Q-r_segs-146"></a>**Q-r_segs-146** R_StoreWallRange pegs walls with the untranslated texture's height, but R_RenderMaskedSegRange uses the height of the animation-translated texture, so an animated texture whose frames differ in height pegs differently in the two places. | r_segs.c:120, 146-148 (masked, translated); r_segs.c:463-465, 581-583 (walls, untranslated) | not stated | not determined from the documents (M5A §1.11 hazard 5 states it; no status given) | yes: use one height consistently | M5A §1.3, §1.11 hazard 5 |
| <a id="Q-r_segs-165"></a>**Q-r_segs-165** maskedtexturecol stores the texture column in a short, so the column truncates to 16 bits, and a column of exactly 32767 equals the MAXSHORT "already drawn" mark and is skipped. | r_segs.c:356 (store), 165 and 185 (sentinel test and set) | not stated | not determined from the documents (M5A §1.11 hazard 6 states it as a port hazard; no status given) | yes: wider type or separate flag | M5A §1.11 hazard 6 |
| <a id="Q-r_segs-181"></a>**Q-r_segs-181** R_RenderMaskedSegRange passes `R_GetColumn(...) - 3` to R_DrawMaskedColumn as a post list, so for a multi-patch composite column the walk reads the previous column's texels (or zone memory before the block) as post headers and runs until some byte reads 0xff. | r_segs.c:181-184; r_things.c:359-385 | the classic "Medusa" garbage on multi-patch masked midtextures (layout dependent) | refused (`MaskedCompositeColumn`, orchestrator application of R3 2026-11-16, "a declared refusal, accepted by the owner 2026-11-16"; a walk leaving its patch lump is `MaskedColumnUndefined`; M5a); no synthetic or real frame reaches either (VAL-M5A) | yes: compose masked columns with post headers | [COMPAT#software-ren…](COMPATIBILITY.md#software-renderer-m5a) ("Vanilla limits (R3)" paragraph); M5A §1.1, §3.3, items 34, 35, 43 and the acceptance paragraph after item 49; VAL-M5A (refusals no frame reaches; MASK experiment); doom-render/src/lib.rs:117-127; doom-render/src/r_data.rs:548, 560, 567; test masked_posts_follow_r_get_column_minus_three (r_data.rs:1283) |
| <a id="Q-r_segs-385"></a>**Q-r_segs-385** R_StoreWallRange returns at once when all 256 drawsegs are used ("don't overflow and crash"), so further wall ranges are not drawn (their columns keep stale pixels), and the later RANGECHECK `ds_p - drawsegs > MAXDRAWSEGS` in R_DrawPlanes can never be true. | r_segs.c:385-387; r_defs.h:55; r_plane.c:376-378 | walls missing from very busy views, showing the previous frame's pixels | reproduced ("`MAXDRAWSEGS` (256) ... drop silently, as the original does", R3, M5a); room DSEGS: first drop at tic 51 (53 at screenblocks 9), per-frame drop counts and world pixels match (VAL-M5A) | yes: larger array | [COMPAT#software-ren…](COMPATIBILITY.md#software-renderer-m5a) ("Vanilla limits (R3)"); M5A §1.8, §1.11 hazard 9, §4.5.4 table; VAL-M5A ("Limit rooms" DSEGS); [ROADMAP#limits-overf…](ROADMAP.md#limits-overflows-and-quirks) ("done (M5a)"); doom-render/src/lib.rs:228-231; doom-render/src/r_segs.rs:14, 250; tools/reference/m5a_wad.py:46 (`dsegs`); tools/reference/m5a_driver.c (frame_drawseg_drops) |
| <a id="Q-r_segs-398"></a>**Q-r_segs-398** R_StoreWallRange sets ML_MAPPED in the linedef's flags for every stored wall range, so drawing a frame changes a gameplay structure (and the bytes a savegame would hold). | r_segs.c:396-398 | nothing in play; lines appear on the automap once seen (the automap reads the bit) | reproduced as presentation state: the renderer keeps its own mapped-line set and "The simulation never has the bit"; the M5a runner masks bit 256 in its game dump (R5, M5a) | yes: keep a separate mapped array | [COMPAT#software-ren…](COMPATIBILITY.md#software-renderer-m5a) ("`ML_MAPPED` (R5)"); M5A summary, §1.9 item 5, §1.11 hazard 13, §3.0, R5; tools/reference/m5a_driver.c header item 3 |

| <a id="Q-s_sound-330"></a>**Q-s_sound-330** Sound pitch (s_sound.c), the status bar face's idle glance (st_stuff.c) and the level-start melt (f_wipe.c) all draw from the single M_Random index, so the face's left/ahead/right glance depends on how many audible sounds started and whether the melt ran. | s_sound.c:330, 340; st_stuff.c:913-919, 992; f_wipe.c:159-166 | the idle face glances differently with and without sound: the genuine face differs from the sound-free pictures in 68,995 of 158,472 real-map frames (COMPAT; another sweep entry quotes 69,139 of 158,966 from the M5b experiment) | under M5b: a declared contract (sound-free, wipe-free, decision S15), so the glance differed from vanilla with sound; closed by M5c: `RandomContract::WithSound` (decisions T6, T14, T16, DDSP version 2) matches the genuine engine on every compared frame; menu hot-key sounds between tics are not modelled | yes: separate presentation generator | [COMPAT#the-status-b…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-status-bar-faces-idle-glance-under-the-m5b-contract-declared-closed-by-m5c); [COMPAT#the-status-b…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-status-bar-faces-idle-glance-is-closed-with-sound-decision-s15-t14); [COMPAT#the-display-…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-display-contract-with-sound-m5c); [COMPAT#the-level-st…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-level-start-wipe-decision-t6); M5B §1.3.6, §2.5 FH1, §3.15 RD1, §4 S15, lines 15 and 3134 (line from the sweep); VAL-M5B ("Wording rule"); M5C; [M3D3G#damage-decis…](M3D3G_REFERENCE.md#damage-decisions-effects-and-rng); tools/reference/m5b_driver.c:24-28, m3h9c_stubs.c:15-19, m5c_driver.c:870-877; commits 745d317, 91152b8, fc722fe |
| <a id="Q-s_sound-349"></a>**Q-s_sound-349** Each start first calls S_StopSound(origin), which frees the first occupied channel with the same origin and accepts NULL, so a new NULL-origin sound (a door "oof", a pickup, a slot-0 switch click) stops an unrelated NULL-origin sound already playing. | s_sound.c:349, 471-484 | a global sound is cut off when another origin-less sound starts (inferred) | reproduced (M5c; script SNDBUSY "sfx_oof with a NULL origin replacing a NULL-origin channel"; sound logic compared with the genuine engine) | unknown: "one sound per origin" design | M5C §1.1.5 (S_StartSoundAtVolume step 7, S_StopSound), line 460 (line from the sweep); VAL-M5C (script SNDBUSY); tools/reference/m5c_wad.py:26-28 |
| <a id="Q-s_sound-364"></a>**Q-s_sound-364** The first start of a sound whose `ds` lump is missing reaches I_GetSfxLumpNum, whose W_GetNumForName ends the game with I_Error, after the pitch random number has been drawn and a channel taken; getsfx's dspistol fallback at start-up does not prevent it. | s_sound.c:364-365; i_sound.c:451-456 (I_GetSfxLumpNum), 213-216 (getsfx fallback) | the game exits with an error on the first start of such a sound | refused (`LumpMissing` at the start that obtains a channel; a start that finds no channel never looks the lump up, as in the original; decision T11, M5c) | yes: use the cached fallback lump | [COMPAT#refusals-ins…](COMPATIBILITY.md#refusals-instead-of-the-originals-errors-decision-t11) (row "A start that obtains a channel, whose `ds` lump is missing"); M5C §1.1.5 step 9, §1.1.14 question 4, §1.3.2 items 8-9 |
| <a id="Q-s_sound-367"></a>**Q-s_sound-367** s_sound.c tests `#ifndef SNDSRV`, a misspelling of SNDSERV defined nowhere, so the "16bit and not pre-cached - wtf?" fprintf is always compiled, and in the shipped build (sound server, no sample data loaded) it prints to stderr for every start that obtains a channel. | s_sound.c:367-381 | nothing in play; a stderr line per sound start in the shipped build | not applicable to the reference: the declared reference is the in-process build (SNDSERV removed, decision T2), where sample data is loaded and nothing prints (M5C §1.1.5 step 10) | yes: correct the spelling | M5C §1.1.0, §1.1.5 (S_StartSoundAtVolume step 10); [COMPAT#the-referenc…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-reference) |
| <a id="Q-s_sound-497"></a>**Q-s_sound-497** Pause touches only music; G_Ticker and gametic keep advancing and S_UpdateSounds still runs every loop, so sound channels (whose "playing" test is `gametic < handle`) keep expiring while the game is paused, and the mixer plays them out. | s_sound.c:497-513; g_game.c:706-711; d_net.c:744-746; p_tick.c:135-136; d_main.c:392; i_sound.c:517-521 | not stated | not determined from the documents (the port's command domain cannot pause: "`BT_SPECIAL` cannot be produced (no pause or save command)", [COMPAT#declared-con…](COMPATIBILITY.md#declared-contracts-witnesses-and-refusals)) | unknown | M5C §1.1.5 (S_PauseSound / S_ResumeSound), §1.1.6 |
| <a id="Q-s_sound-634"></a>**Q-s_sound-634** S_SetSfxVolume accepts any volume 0..127, so a config-file snd_SfxVolume of 16..127 is used although the menu slider offers only 0..15 (outside 0..127 is fatal at S_Init). | s_sound.c:172, 631-639 (634-635) | not stated (sounds louder than the menu's maximum; map 8's distance rule changes with it) | not determined from the documents (the port refuses a volume outside 0..127, [COMPAT#what-the-sou…](COMPATIBILITY.md#what-the-sound-code-refuses); acceptance of 16..127 is not stated) | unknown | M5C §1.1.13 item 9, §1.1.5 (S_SetSfxVolume) |

| <a id="Q-s_sound-767"></a>**Q-s_sound-767** S_AdjustSoundParams subtracts coordinates and adds the distances in plain int, which is undefined on overflow when listener and source are 32,768 units or more apart on an axis, or the sum exceeds 2^31-1 (both offsets around 16,384 units). | s_sound.c:767-771 | nothing observable on real maps (they would need to span more than 16,384 units) | not determined from the documents (M5C §1.1.13 item 2: gcc at -O0 wraps, observed in run A) | yes: wider or unsigned arithmetic | M5C §1.1.13 item 2, §1.1.9 |
| <a id="Q-s_sound-773"></a>**Q-s_sound-773** S_AdjustSoundParams tests `gamemap != 8`, so on every map numbered 8 (E1M8-E4M8, MAP08) nothing is out of earshot, and its map-8 volume formula rises with distance: at the default volume 8, a sound is 8 near and up to 15 far. | s_sound.c:773-777, 800-808 | on map 8 of each episode, distant sounds are audible and louder than near ones | reproduced (audible quirk 5, decision T13, M5c) | yes: drop the map-8 special case | [COMPAT#audible-quir…](COMPATIBILITY.md#audible-quirks-of-this-source-reproduced-as-they-are-decision-t13) (row 5); M5C §1.1.5 (S_AdjustSoundParams steps 3, 8) |
| <a id="Q-s_sound-785"></a>**Q-s_sound-785** For a source bearing at or left of the listener's facing, S_AdjustSoundParams computes `angle + (0xffffffff - listener->angle)`, one less than the true difference, so a bearing equal to the facing gives 0xffffffff instead of 0 (a negative sine, separation 129 instead of 128). | s_sound.c:785-788, 793 | a sound straight ahead is panned one step to one side (not audible in practice, line from the sweep) | reproduced (M5c; "the genuine BARON1 stream's first row has the same 129", crates/doom-sound/tests/logic_rules.rs:396-398) | yes: plain unsigned subtraction | M5C §1.1.5 (S_AdjustSoundParams step 5); doom-sound/src/logic.rs:193; doom-sound/tests/logic_rules.rs:396-398 |
| <a id="Q-s_sound-842"></a>**Q-s_sound-842** S_getChannel would stop a channel that has the same non-NULL origin, but its only caller has just run S_StopSound(origin), which freed any such channel, so the branch never fires. | s_sound.c:842-846; call order s_sound.c:349-352 | nothing observable in play | reproduced as unreachable (M5C §1.1.5 S_getChannel item 4, "Inference, proved from the code"; crates/doom-sound/src/logic.rs:233) | yes: delete it | M5C §1.1.5 (S_getChannel item 4), §1.1.10 item 3; doom-sound/src/logic.rs:233 |
| <a id="Q-s_sound-853"></a>**Q-s_sound-853** When every channel is busy, S_getChannel evicts the first channel whose table priority is greater than or equal to the new sound's (a lower number is more important), so a new sound replaces an equally important one, and the comment "Look for lower priority" means lower importance. | s_sound.c:850-860 (853-854) | with all channels busy, a new sound replaces an equally important playing one rather than being refused | reproduced (M5c: the allocation and priority cases match the genuine tests, VAL-M5C `tests/logic_rules.rs`; COMPAT audible quirk 8) | yes: use `>` (changes behaviour) | M5C §1.1.4; VAL-M5C (sound logic rules); [COMPAT#audible-quir…](COMPATIBILITY.md#audible-quirks-of-this-source-reproduced-as-they-are-decision-t13) (row 8) |

- **Q-s_sound-327**: kept apart from the draw-order row: one is the audible pitch, the other the random-index consequence.

- **Q-s_sound-364**: M5C counts 41 missing ids for DOOM.WAD (§1.1.14 question 4); none is started by Episode 1 play as far as the investigation found (COMPAT).
- **Q-s_sound-726**: dead code.
- **Q-s_sound-773**: merges map8-sound-attenuation-inverted and map8-no-clip-and-louder-when-far.

| <a id="Q-wadread-206"></a>**Q-wadread-206** The shipped sound server finds lumps by a forward scan (first match wins, where w_wad.c scans backward so PWADs win), opens its own IWAD by fixed names under DOOMWADDIR and ignores the game's -file PWADs. | sndserv/wadread.c:206-210; sndserv/soundsrv.c:320-366 (verified); soundsrv.c:430, 478, 629 (channelstart, as cited, not verified) | replacement sounds in PWADs are not heard through the shipped server (inferred) | not yet ported — out of scope: the declared reference is the in-process build (decision T2); M5C §3.2.1 item 5 ("[inf]"; not built or run) | yes: scan backward and take the game's WAD list | M5C §3.2.1 item 5, §1.3.11 Q1; [COMPAT#the-referenc…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-reference) |
| <a id="Q-wadread-245"></a>**Q-wadread-245** In the sound server, a missing `ds` lump makes loadlump return 0 without setting `*size`, so getsfx computes the padding from an uninitialised int and reallocs a NULL pointer to that garbage size (undefined); there is no dspistol fallback as in the in-process getsfx. | sndserv/wadread.c:212-217 (not found), 239, 245-251 | a crash or garbage for a missing sound lump with the shipped server (for example Doom II names in a Doom 1 IWAD) | not yet ported — out of scope (the server is not the reference, decision T2; M5C §3.2.1 item 5, read only, "nothing built or run") | yes: initialise size and fall back | M5C §3.2.1 |

| <a id="Q-st_lib-99"></a>**Q-st_lib-99** STlib_drawNum erases its field with a rectangle sized from digit 0's width and height and ignoring patch offsets, so a digit patch drawn outside that box (another offset or size) leaves pixels behind when the number changes. | st_lib.c:99-100, 120-125 | a row of stale pixels beside a changing number when a digit patch is offset or larger than digit 0 (the synthetic font's digit 7 is one row low on purpose) | reproduced (M5b: the synthetic STTNUM7 leaves "a row ... behind when the number changes, as the genuine code does"; goldens match, M5B §2.4.2) | yes: erase the old digits' rectangles | M5B §1.4.8 step 2, §1.4.12 item 8, §2.4.2; VAL-M5B; tools/reference/m5b_gfx.py:15-21 |
| <a id="Q-st_lib-138"></a>**Q-st_lib-138** STlib_drawNum draws at most `width` digits (`while (num && numdigits--)`), so a value with more digits than the field shows only its low digits, with no sign of truncation. | st_lib.c:96, 138-143 | the high digits of an oversized value vanish (not reachable with Episode 1 values, per the documents' value ranges) | reproduced (the widget logic is ported and compared on 59 items, M5b) — the truncation case itself is not stated as exercised | yes: clamp or widen the field | M5B §1.4.8 step 2, §1.4.12 item 8 |
| <a id="Q-st_lib-212"></a>**Q-st_lib-212** STlib_updateMultIcon erases the old icon by copying from the background buffer, which holds STBAR but not STARMS (drawn straight to the foreground), using the previous patch's offsets and size, so after a weapon-slot digit changes the bar can differ from what a full redraw would show. | st_lib.c:212-240 (225-236); st_stuff.c:1308-1324 (arms widgets) | stray pixels or a different background behind weapon digits after pickups (two pixels remain in M5STARM after a screenblocks 11 to 10 switch) | reproduced (VAL-M5B step 3: goldens match the genuine engine; "A port that computes the bar from state alone is wrong after a weapon-digit change") | yes: keep STARMS in the background | M5B §1.4.8 step 6, §1.4.13, §1.4.14; VAL-M5B step 3 |
| <a id="Q-st_lib-223"></a>**Q-st_lib-223** STlib_updateMultIcon does nothing when the value turns to -1 (the old icon stays in the foreground and oldinum keeps the old index), and nothing when the bar is off. | st_lib.c:221-223 | a lost key icon would stay drawn (cannot happen inside a level; keys clear only between levels) | reproduced (the widget logic is ported and compared on 59 items, VAL-M5B step 3) | yes: erase on -1 | M5B §1.4.8 step 6, §1.4.11 item 4, §1.4.14 |

| <a id="Q-st_stuff-819"></a>**Q-st_stuff-819** The face computes diffang as an unsigned difference without folding it, so an attacker just across the 0/360 wrap from the view direction gives a difference near a full turn and the face turns instead of looking head-on. | st_stuff.c:819-830, 836 | an enemy almost straight ahead but across the angle wrap makes the face glance left or right instead of snarling head-on | reproduced (VAL-M5B step 3; M5B §1.4.14 "an unfolded diffang"; reviewers found every table and threshold equal to the C) | yes: fold the difference | M5B §1.4.4 step 3.3, §1.4.14; VAL-M5B ("First pair of reviews") |

## Recorded in only one place and in no note

The owner asked to see anything that is described as a quirk in only one place
(a source comment, a test name, a tool, an archive file or a commit message)
and nowhere in an investigation note, an evidence record or COMPATIBILITY, so
that it is clear what was never properly recorded. For every row the merge
re-checked with broad greps over `docs/*.md` and `docs/validation/*.md` (the
archive excluded); several sweepers' "found only here" claims were wrong on
re-check and are not repeated. What is left is below.

**Rows whose only source is a source comment** (searched in `docs/*.md` and `docs/validation/*.md`, not the archive; the notes are the investigation notes, COMPATIBILITY and the evidence records):

- `Q-p_lights-83`: a fire flicker's lower bound can sit above its upper bound. Only a comment in `fuzz/fuzz_targets/player_specials.rs:168-172`. Searches: `minlight`, `fireflicker`, `fire flicker`, `flicker`, `& 64` / `maxtime`, `exceed`, `minlight + 16`, `p_lights.c:83`. The notes give only the formula, the special-17 listing, the field description and an "ordered" envelope check; none says `minlight` can exceed `maxlight`.
- `Q-r_data-394`: a single-patch column whose patch is lump 0 reads unwritten composite bytes. Only a comment in `crates/doom-render/src/r_data.rs:267-272` (and a test comment). Searches: `lump 0`, `lump zero`, `lump > 0`, `lump number 0`, `lump index 0`, `LumpZero`, `collump[x] >= 0`, `first lump of the directory`; the only hits state the `lump > 0` test, not the consequence.
- `Q-p_sight-106`: the comment of `P_InterceptVector2` names the wrong callers. Only the C source comment itself. Searches: `only called by`, `addthings and addlines`, `addlines traverser`.

**Rows with a part recorded in only one place:**

- `Q-p_enemy-738`: the consequence "a Baron that can never move keeps movecount nonzero and never fires" is stated only in `tools/reference/m4b_wad.py:362-365` (searched `never fires`, `can never move`, `keeps movecount`). The gate itself is in the notes.
- `Q-p_enemy-865`: the contradiction between the comment "keep firing unless target got out of sight" and the code is the reading of an earlier sweep of the archive; no note states it (searched `keep firing`, `got out of sight`).
- `Q-p_map-189`: the checklist row in ROADMAP and the commit that added it (2eb9398) are the only places; no investigation note and no COMPATIBILITY section describes the behaviour. COMPATIBILITY records only that E1M7 on skill 3 is refused at load for a reason "not yet known".
- `Q-p_map-618`: the extra `ANG180` in `P_HitSlideLine` is described as source in M3H5, but no document says it can reverse the slide; the reversal values are the sweep's own computation.
- `Q-g_game-1421`: with `-fast`, every later `G_InitNew` halves the tics again (the condition tests `fastparm` alone); only the single halving is in a note (M3D3F). Searched `halv`, `>>= 1`, `fastparm`.
- `Q-i_sound-334`, `Q-i_sound-143`, `Q-i_sound-364`: the i_sound.c comments that contradict the code ("(unused)" at 339, "(not used)" at 494, the "clamp volume" comment at 363, the "unused" pitch table). The notes record the code, not that the comments are wrong; commits 5700cb5 and cff3ed4 mention the pitch comments.
- `Q-p_mobj-331`: the comment at p_mobj.c:288-290 about the lost-soul momz negation describes an older statement order than the code (the negation at 291-295 comes before the zeroing at 297-310). No document discusses it (searched `kinda useless`, `somebody left`, `p_mobj.c:29`).
- `Q-p_saveg-303`: `tracer` is restored unchanged on load by the same raw copy (p_saveg.c:300), keeping a pointer value from the saving session. No document says so (searched `tracer` with save, unarchive, p_saveg).
- `Q-p_mobj-794`: p_mobj.c:627 is cited by no document (the angle row has other sources).

**Readings that are the register builder's or an earlier sweep's own, in no document** (each is in the row's note or the sentence below; none is presented as an observation):
`Q-g_game-74` (the check follows the writes), `Q-g_game-815` (the consequence for the menu pause), `Q-p_doors-118` (the `open` type is also missing from the down branch's switch), `Q-p_enemy-512` (the single-player case), `Q-p_enemy-693` (`actor->angle &= (7<<29)` has the same signed-shift form), `Q-p_enemy-802` (the chaingunner plays `sfx_shotgn`), `Q-p_mobj-900` (no document states a player-visible effect), `Q-p_mobj-758` (the consequence of an unknown THING type is not stated in words), `Q-p_map-1290` (a C observation of the item case exists only in tools; the wire-20 sentence was not updated).

## Not traced to a C line

None is left. The sweep carried candidates that cited no line or that the
sweeper could not verify; the merge traced every one by opening the C:
the dying barrel (`info.c:944-948`, `p_inter.c:675`, `p_enemy.c:1588`), the
`A_Explode` freed-memory read (`p_enemy.c:1600`, `p_map.c:1227`), "only the
last motion event of a tic counts" (`g_game.c:576-580`), "fire can stick"
(`i_video.c:218-247`), the stale `attacker` pointer (the absent clear in
`P_SpawnPlayer` and `G_PlayerFinishLevel`, the writer `p_inter.c:874` and the
reader `p_user.c:200-205`), the sector sound-origin overflow
(`p_setup.c:556-557`), the diagonal-wall "sliver" reading quirks
(`p_maputl.c:91-99`, `139-152`), the inverting door (`p_doors.c:497-498`), the
shared `M_Random` index (`s_sound.c:330`), the unpadded masked-midtexture read
(`r_draw.c:142`), and others. Entries judged
to cite no behaviour of the original at all are in the dropped list, not here.

## Dropped, and why

What the merge dropped as not a quirk of the original: plain design, faults
of the Rust port, harness artefacts, aggregate or duplicate list entries and
documentation slips. Each says why. The row text is as the merger wrote it
(the sweep's entry names are kept so a reader can search the notes).
One item is left for the owner to rule in or out:

- `z_zone.c:440` (`Z_ChangeTag`'s owner-pointer test, found in a commit
  message): exact on the original's 32-bit target; it misbehaves only when
  the harness builds the C for LP64, where a declared adapter compares the
  whole pointer. It is a portability fault of building the old C on a modern
  host, not a behaviour of linuxdoom-1.10 as shipped, so it is not a row. If
  the register is to include LP64 portability faults, it would sit with the
  other LP64 adapters (`r_draw.c:464`, `r_data.c`, `g_game.c:495-496`).

**Chunk 1 (d_main.c, d_net.c, doomdef.h, f_wipe.c, g_game.c, hu_lib.c, hu_stuff.c, i_sound.c, i_system.c):**

1. skill-argument-is-one-more-than-the-skill-value — plain design: the command line counts skills from 1 like the menu, and the demo header stores the 0-based skill_t; M5B note 2.7 records it as a naming difference, not a defect.
2. actions-have-no-original-ordinal — a port representation rule (the Rust ActionId never serialises a C address); the `(actionf_v)(-1)` removal sentinel has no effect in play.
3. nochange-sentinel-ten-numweapons-nine — plain design: an enum sentinel placed after the count.
4. nochange-sentinel-is-10-after-numweapons-9 — duplicate of 3, same reason.
5. wipe-colour-crossfade-unreachable — dead code with no effect (the sweeper noted it as likely not a quirk).
6. noblit-parsed-never-read — a variable set and never read; no effect.
7. hu-lastautomapactive-is-dead-state — a static written and never read; no effect.
8. steptable-clear-loop-commented-out — a commented-out clearing loop whose state static zero initialisation already gives; no effect.
9. i-sound-errno-redeclared — a build-portability issue of the source with a modern libc (handled by declared one-line adapters), not behaviour.
10. weapon-key-nine-never-tested — plain design: the loop stops before wp_supershotgun because P_PlayerThink selects it through key 3 (p_user.c:300-303, verified); keys 9 and 0 are simply unbound.

**Chunk 2 (i_video.c, info.c, info.h, m_bbox.c, m_fixed.c, m_menu.c, m_misc.c, m_random.c, p_ceilng.c, p_doors.c, p_enemy.c):**

1. info.c `firepistol-cadence-nineteen-vs-fourteen`: plain design. The pistol's first and held-fire intervals follow from the state durations (info.c:149-152, verified); nothing about them is a bug, accident or surprise.
2. info.c `player-type-has-missile-state-reference`: no behaviour of the original. MT_PLAYER's missilestate S_PLAY_ATK1 (info.c:1120, verified) is never used through A_Chase for a player; the finding concerns a Rust native copy (harness/port), which clears it.
3. info.h `info-fields-are-c-long`: plain design. state_t's frame/tics/misc fields are C long (info.h:1148-1154, verified); every value in the table fits i32, so no outcome depends on the width.
4. m_random.c `rndtable-never-returns-40`: an evidence limit, not a behaviour. The table lacks 40 and 38, so a `< 40` boundary cannot be exercised by real draws; no outcome of the original differs. (The fact is kept as a note on the A_CPosRefire row.)
5. m_random.c `painchance-lut-lacks-179-180`: the same kind of evidence limit (no 179 or 180 in the table).
6. m_random.c `rndtable-has-no-40-and-no-179-180`: duplicate of 4 and 5.
7. p_enemy.c `lookforplayers-rear-accept-within-melee`: plain design; the source comment says "if real close, react anyway" (p_enemy.c:547-549).
8. p_enemy.c `alook-wake-sound-random-groups`: plain design (random choice among the see sounds, p_enemy.c:635-651).
9. p_enemy.c `chase-reactiontime-does-not-delay`: plain design; reactiontime gates only the missile decision (p_enemy.c:212-213, 676-677).
10. p_enemy.c `chase-justattacked-nightmare-skips-newchasedir`: plain design of nightmare/fast (p_enemy.c:719).
11. p_enemy.c `chase-active-sound-draw-always`: plain design; a 3-in-256 chance is a draw by nature (p_enemy.c:771-775).
12. p_enemy.c `sposattack-shared-aim-sequential-pellets`: plain design; one aim per volley, pellets resolved in order (p_enemy.c:835-842), as for the player's shotgun.
13. p_enemy.c `skullattack-sound-no-zero-guard`: no behaviour of the original; the only user (MT_SKULL) has a nonzero attack sound, so the missing guard never acts (p_enemy.c:1431).

Not dropped although its sweeper suggested it: p_enemy.c `reachable-pdiehi-only-commercial` is merged into the hard-death-scream row, which other sweeps recorded as a quirk of the C.

**Chunk 3 (p_floor.c, p_inter.c, p_lights.c, p_local.h, p_user.c):**

- `bonuscount-has-no-cap`: plain design. The counter is decremented every tic and the palette index is clamped (st_stuff.c:1031-1034); no overflow is reachable. The "no cap" remark is a Rust bound comment (crates/doom-render/src/d_display.rs:640-641).
- `3. P_GiveAmmo case am_misl has no break`: no behavioural effect; the fall-through lands in an empty `default: break;`. The entry itself says "Effect: none".
- `medikit-and-armor-names-gotmega-is-blue-armor`: identifier naming only. GOTMEGA's text is "Picked up the MegaArmor!" (d_englsh.h:81), the blue armor's own name; nothing differs in play.
- `health-bonus-collects-at-cap-and-counts`: plain design ("Bonuses are always collected, even at the cap", COMPATIBILITY#original-health-and-armor-bonuses; the sweeper marked it intended).
- `soulsphere-always-picked-up`: plain design (the sweeper marked it design).
- `player-mirrored-health-vs-mobj-health`: plain design, nothing observable; M4B §7 finding 20 records that the clamped player health and the signed body health agree on every `> 0` test.
- `damage-armor-saved-capped-clears-type-before-subtract`: plain design of armor absorption; clearing the type before the subtraction has no effect on the result.
- `xdeath-needs-health-strictly-below-minus-spawnhealth`: a boundary choice, not a defect (the sweeper says so); the same E entry's tics part is merged into p_inter.c:726.
- `player-attacker-null-does-not-clear-target`: plain design; `P_DeathThink` already handles a null or self attacker (p_user.c:200), and a player body's `target` is not used to choose anything here.

**Chunk 4 (p_map.c, p_maputl.c):**

- `checkposition-center-sector-vs-extreme-sector`: while a box straddles sectors, the centre's sector can differ from the sector giving the highest floor. Dropped as plain design: the C comment at p_map.c:402-405 states the intent ("The base floor / ceiling is from the subsector that contains the point. Any contacted lines the step closer together will adjust them"), and the sweep itself rated it "inherent to the design".
- Part of `rocket-direct-hit-then-splash-and-post-explosion-half-step`: "a rocket that hits a thing damages it in PIT_CheckThing and then again in the explosion's P_RadiusAttack" (p_map.c:323-324, p_enemy.c:1600). Dropped as plain design (the rocket's impact-plus-splash damage). The entry's other half (the post-explosion half step) is the p_mobj.c:150 row, so the entry is counted as merged, not dropped.

**Chunk 5 (p_mobj.c, p_plats.c, p_pspr.c, p_saveg.c, p_setup.c):**

Nine entries, each with the reason (every C line named was opened):

1. `setmobjstate-null-no-field-update` — plain design: entering S_NULL removes the actor; leaving its tics, sprite and frame unassigned has no consequence (nothing observable).
2. `corpse-friction-exception` — plain design: the comment states the intent ("do not stop sliding if halfway off a step with some momentum").
3. `corpse-friction-exception-at-ledges` — duplicate of 2, same reason.
4. `player-stop-threshold-angle-only` — plain design: a player stops below STOPSPEED only without forward or side input; turning is not movement.
5. `nogravity-momz-survives-explosion` — not a quirk: NOGRAVITY simply skips gravity, and P_ExplodeMissile zeroes momz at p_mobj.c:92, so nothing "survives"; M3H2 itself makes no observation claim (momz is always zero there).
6. `terminal-countkill-respawn-gate` — plain design: the nightmare / -respawn timer (12*35 updates, leveltime&31, P_Random() > 4) as written; no accident is described.
7. `missile-launcher-and-bfg-do-not-autofire` — plain design: the comment says "the missile launcher and bfg do not auto fire". The entry's disagreement is a documentation slip only: M4A 3.5.3 says G_PlayerReborn sets attackdown, docs/validation/M4A.md says usedown; g_game.c:822 sets both (`p->usedown = p->attackdown = true;`).
8. `chaingun-flash-state-offset-arithmetic` — plain design: the flash frame is chosen by offset in the contiguous state table, which is how the table is laid out; nothing observable.
9. `alower-does-not-clamp-height-for-a-live-player` — no behaviour: the unclamped height is overwritten at once by P_BringUpWeapon (`sy = WEAPONBOTTOM`, p_pspr.c:151) or the psprite is set to S_NULL (p_pspr.c:409), so it is never displayed or read. The A_Lower half of batch E's `psprite-lower-no-clamp-and-flash-birth-tic` is dropped for the same reason; its flash half is merged into p_pspr.c:875.

Parts of merged entries not carried as quirks (the entries themselves are merged, not dropped): the extra `1 + P_Random() % tics` start-frame draw (p_mobj.c:787-788, E `spawnmapthing-extra-random-and-order`, intended randomisation); A_Saw's hit test reading the aim trace's `linetarget` (G1b, ordinary design); "puff and blood before damage" (E `shoot-effects-before-damage-and-jitter`, an order in p_map.c, left to the p_map.c rows); the floor `newspecial` and door `topcountdown` allocations (H2, G1a, left to the p_floor.c and p_doors.c rows).

**Chunk 6 (p_sight.c, p_spec.c, p_switch.c, p_telept.c, p_tick.c, p_user.c, r_bsp.c, r_data.c, r_defs.h):**

- E `crossbspnode-side2-visits-both-children` (p_sight.c:257-290, verified): plain design. Mapping a start side 2 to 0 and crossing the other child when the target side differs is what the C comment at p_sight.c:275 says it intends ("an 'on' should cross both sides"); "preserving the actual source order" is a port requirement, not a quirk.
- E `checksight-reject-same-sector` (p_sight.c:313-326, verified): plain design. REJECT is a precomputed table the engine is meant to obey; a set bit hiding a same-sector monster is map data, not engine behaviour (the M3D3A note itself calls asymmetric bits "legitimate test inputs").
- H2 `thinker-function-pointer-sentinel` (p_tick.c:108, verified): plain design idiom with no behavioural effect; the archive entry is a harness observation ("GNU compiler-profile observations"), not a quirk of play.
- E `playermove-run-entry-only-from-s-play` (p_user.c:166-170, verified): plain design (the run animation starts only from the standing state, so pain is not interrupted; the sweeper also judged "no" for a small change).
- H2 `dead-player-gets-no-hint-to-press-use` (p_user.c:227-228, verified as the BT_USE reborn test): plain design of the original (no on-screen hint exists to be missing); the source is a usability note of the port.
- G2 `composite-texture-over-64k-errors` (r_data.c:365-369, verified): plain design, a deliberate checked limit with an error message that keeps 16-bit column offsets valid.

**Chunk 7 (r_draw.c, r_main.c, r_plane.c, r_segs.c, r_sky.c, r_things.c, s_sound.c, sounds.c, sounds.h, st_lib.c, st_stuff.c, st_stuff.h, tables.c, v_video.c, w_wad.c, sndserv/wadread.c, wi_stuff.c, z_zone.c):**

1. renderer-vanilla-limits-and-undefined-reads — an aggregate list entry with no C lines; each item it lists is its own row here (r_plane.c:245, r_plane.c:384, r_bsp.c:88, r_segs.c:385, r_things.c:326, r_things.c:815, r_draw.c:142, r_draw.c:274, r_main.c:870, r_segs.c:181, r_segs.c:81), and COMPAT is cited in each. (The other two aggregate entries, renderer limits (checklist rows) and renderer-limits-refuse-or-drop-silently, give C lines and sources and were merged into those rows rather than dropped.)
2. makefile-flags-line-numbers-differ — a documentation slip, not a quirk of the original: M5C §1.1.0 cites the Makefile CFLAGS line as Makefile:9 (correct) and §1.3.0 item 1 as Makefile:8 with CC :6 and LIBS :10 (actual CC :7, CFLAGS :9, LIBS :11; the sweeper verified). Recorded here for the documentation owner; no document is changed (see "Where the documents disagree").
3. adjustsoundparams-volume-arithmetic-overflows — a Rust-port fault: doom-sound's public `adjust_sound_params` overflowed and panicked in a debug build for a volume outside 0..127 (COMPAT#what-the-sound-code-refuses: "before, such a value overflowed and panicked in a debug build. The session's own configuration is always checked, so no game reaches it"). In the original every value of snd_SfxVolume reaches S_AdjustSoundParams only after S_SetSfxVolume's 0..127 check (s_sound.c:172, 634-635; the menu also calls it), and 127 x 1040 cannot overflow, so the C has no reachable overflow at s_sound.c:812-815. The genuine config-range behaviour is the row s_sound.c:634.
4. zchangetag-tests-owner-pointer-truncated-to-unsigned — exact on the original's 32-bit target; it misbehaves only when the harness builds the C for LP64, where a declared adapter compares the whole pointer (docs/M3H9C_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md lines 847, 1066). A harness/portability artefact, not a behaviour of linuxdoom-1.10 as shipped. Left for the owner to rule in or out: reinstate it if the register is to include LP64 portability faults (it would then sit with the other LP64 adapters, r_draw.c:464, r_data.c, g_game.c:495-496).
5. "Six named files" — a coverage listing of comment lines read, not a quirk entry.
6. "Other files of G1a's range" — a coverage listing, not a quirk entry.

## Where the documents disagree

Every note the merge recorded where one document differs from another, from
the C or from the line it cites, with which side matches the C where the merger
could tell, including corrected line numbers. Nothing is fixed here: no note,
record, comment, test or tool was edited. The sentences are the merger's,
grouped by row id (in text: the ids are not linked because the documentation
link check resolves only heading anchors).

- `Q-d_main-150`: the tool wording "more than 64 unprocessed events overwrite the oldest" and the note's "all 64 are silently lost" describe two counts of the same wrap; both match the C (64 exactly empties the ring, 65 leaves one event).
- `Q-d_main-331`: the M5C note gives 28-42 dropped tics; not checked further here.
- `Q-d_net-669`: the note's probe `loop.c` is "a model, not the genuine engine".
- `Q-doomdef_h-80`: tools/reference/m5c_mixer_build.py:73, 230 and m5c_build.py:75, 99 still say decision T2 is "open", while [COMPATIBILITY#the-referenc…](COMPATIBILITY.md#the-reference) states T2 as decided (owner rulings 2026-11-18); the tool text is stale.
- `Q-doomdef_h-80`: The M5C note cites doomdef.h:76-83 for the comment; the define is at 84.
- `Q-g_game-74`: that the check follows the writes is the register builder's reading of g_game.c:1284-1311; ROADMAP states only the I_Error.
- `Q-g_game-495`: M5B note 2.2.5 item 1 reports the strict32 build differing from strict on 2 prototype scripts until an adapter is declared, while M5B record step 2 says the strict32 build is "identical on 251 of 251, with the genuine text and no adapter"; the record does not say where the adapter sits (S11 suggests the LP64 builds). tools/reference/m5b_input_scripts.py:219 cites the clear as g_game.c:489-496 (the memsets are at 495-496).
- `Q-g_game-576`: not to be confused with the i_video.c mask slip that leaves fire on (M5B note S4 item 4), a different cause.
- `Q-g_game-579`: M5B fact 35 cites g_game.c:579-580, 405-411.
- `Q-g_game-672`: M4A note 3.10.3 (and an earlier note) call it message-only presentation; section 14 of the same note corrects that it is captured state.
- `Q-g_game-1421`: M3D3F cites g_game.c:1423 (the loop); the block is 1421-1436.
- `Q-g_game-1515`: [ROADMAP#demos](ROADMAP.md#demos) cites only g_game.c:1508-1509 (the q test) next to "the buffer limit"; the limit itself is at 1515-1520.
- `Q-hu_stuff-454`: ROADMAP cites hu_stuff.c:453-460 for the comment; it spans 454-461.
- `Q-hu_stuff-522`: the note marks the order an inference from the call sequence.
- `Q-i_sound-213`: The H1b sweep calls the fallback dead code; it does run at load, it is only never heard.
- `Q-i_sound-479`: The i_sound.c comment at 465-466 says priority "is ignored".
- `Q-i_sound-489`: the note calls it "a pure artefact of the enum order".
- `Q-i_sound-505`: COMPATIBILITY cites I_UpdateSoundParams as 675-686; the function spans 674-688.
- `Q-i_sound-517`: M5C note 1.1.15 says the 65535 wrap was "not tested by running"; M5C record step 2c lists a handle-wrap mixer scenario (the counter, not the s_sound consequence).
- `Q-i_sound-811`: M5C note 1.3.2 item 12 says "every chaingun shot plays exactly one frame", while the same note (1.1.3) and COMPATIBILITY row 6 say no game code starts it; a grep of linuxdoom-1.10 finds sfx_chgun only in sounds.c, so COMPATIBILITY matches the C and the 1.3.2 wording does not.
- `Q-i_sound-811`: COMPAT cites i_sound.c:801-811; the division is at 811 (M5C §1.1.13 item 11).
- `Q-i_video-197`: M5B fact 27 cites i_video.c:203-214 and item 44 cites 199-214; the declaration is at 197.
- `Q-i_video-218`: COMPAT names no cause; M5B names the bit-8 slip; not contradictory.
- `Q-i_video-253`: COMPAT says only "purely horizontal moves are dropped"; M5B (and the C) drop any move whose x or y is on the centre line, i.e. purely vertical moves too.
- `Q-i_video-253`: COMPAT is narrower, not contradictory; M5B matches the C.
- `Q-i_video-361`: M5B §1.4.14 cites i_video.c:361-372; the block ends at 374.
- `Q-i_video-799`: M5B §4 "Owner rulings" S2 and S6 say that with miniquad keys held across Alt-Tab "stay held until pressed again", while M5B §2.7 and [COMPAT#input](COMPATIBILITY.md#input)/#platform-limits say the library reports minimise/focus loss and the app releases every key; the later text (§2.7, COMPAT) corrects the earlier one.
- `Q-info-40`: M5A cites r_things.c:191 for the loop; the `while (*check != NULL)` is at 191 inside 190-192.
- `Q-m_bbox-51`: M5C §1.2.6.2 labels the consequence an inference and adds that the origin uses a signed /2 (truncation toward zero, implementation-defined in C89) and that soundorg.z and soundorg.thinker are never set (unused by the call sites).
- `Q-m_misc-282-2`: M5C §1.1.2 says only that it is "not yet checked against the port's choices".
- `Q-p_doors-118`: from the C, the `open` type is also missing from the down branch's pastdest switch and would behave the same if reversed (the register builder's reading; no document says so).
- `Q-p_doors-416`: M4A §3.14 item 2 says it refines M3h8 §2.4.
- `Q-p_floor-73`: M3H8 cites the last-step tests as 67/96/134/168, one line before the `if` lines 68/97/135/169.
- `Q-p_floor-97`: M4A cites the raiseFloor case as 318-327; the case labels are 317-319 and the destination 323-328 (raiseFloorCrush falls through at 317-318).
- `Q-p_floor-307`: M3H8 notes the destination folds from -500 (p_spec.c), so a neighbour below -500 gives -492 (that fold belongs to the p_spec.c rows).
- `Q-p_inter-281`: COMPATIBILITY's Keys row says only that a second card of a colour adds just its `BONUSADD` (the early return at 278-279); the reset by assignment is stated in M4A §3.6.1.
- `Q-p_inter-476`: numbering differs: STATE_FORMAT calls it Message7 (the DSIM pickup-message code, where 6 is the medikit), COMPATIBILITY's M5b section, M5B and validation/M5B.md call it code 6 (the M5b message numbering); both name the same C message.
- `Q-p_inter-476`: M5B's first note marks the reasoning as inference; its later finding states it from the source lines.
- `Q-p_inter-808`: M4A §3.5.2 first described the rule as the saw's own effect; its §14 corrects it to the ready weapon, so rocket damage also thrusts nothing after a switch.
- `Q-p_inter-808`: The C matches the correction.
- `Q-p_inter-897`: M3H3 records that the first draft said 25 and the independent review found 23.
- `Q-p_local_h-51`: tools/reference/m3h9b_reference.py:98 says the p_map.c:1221 sanitizer adapter is "not needed by these builds"; m3h9c_build.py lists the same adapter without saying so (per the sweep).
- `Q-p_local_h-51`: That is about adapter necessity, not the C.
- `Q-p_local_h-51`: The comment at p_local.h:48-50 says MAXRADIUS is "for precalculated sector block boxes", not blast range.
- `Q-p_local_h-154`: COMPATIBILITY's wire-17 section heading is "classic-map scale"; the sweep's "M3h6 owner decision 2026-11-02" wording is from the M3H6 note.
- `Q-p_map-67`: M4A (§3.13 row, line 1215) says the overflow "writes past the array into numspechit and following globals"; M4B correction 8 (line 872) and T9.1 (757-760) say declaration order does not fix the memory layout, so what is overwritten cannot be known from the source; the C (two uninitialised file-scope definitions, p_map.c:69-70) matches M4B.
- `Q-p_map-67`: M4B T9.1 (line 762) says the address-variant abort at p_map.c:242 was "expected but not observed"; the later evidence record (validation/M4B.md:76) observed it (`p_map.c:242:9: runtime error: index 8 out of bounds`), so the note's sentence is the earlier state.
- `Q-p_map-81`: M4B correction 1 (line 855-858) corrects M4A §3.3.2: corpses are not shootable, so they are never stomped and never block a monster teleport.
- `Q-p_map-189`: same root as the destination-box-only test of the next row (only the destination box is ever tested); kept separate because the player-visible effect and the status differ.
- `Q-p_map-214`: the M3H9 note's first text and the docs before review finding 6 said a blocking two-sided 48 "never enters `spechit`" and that fireballs cross it "with no record of their own"; validation/M3H9.md finding 6 confirms that was wrong for missiles, and the C matches the correction.
- `Q-p_map-252`: stale line in a Rust test comment: player_e1m1_tests.rs:951-953 cites p_map.c:345; the final `return !(thing->flags & MF_SOLID);` is at 342.
- `Q-p_map-299-2`: M4A §3.4.2 (line 850) says "baron balls do not hurt barons"; M4B correction 6 says the Baron is not immune to other Barons through splash.
- `Q-p_map-299-2`: The C matches M4B (the splash's source is the barrel's `target`, p_enemy.c:1600, and p_map.c:1194 applies no species test).
- `Q-p_map-492`: the documents cite p_mobj.c:416-431 for the thinker order; P_MobjThinker starts at 415 and the calls are at 418-431 (same lines in substance).
- `Q-p_map-502`: M4B correction 2 corrects M4A §3.3.3: the loop stops whenever P_TeleportMove is called, a blocked monster teleport included, and not when EV_Teleport returns earlier (missile, back side, no landing); the C (numspechit reset at 152, before the stomp loop) matches M4B.
- `Q-p_map-502-2`: M4A §3.14 item 5 / §3.3.3 presented "numspechit after a teleport is −1" as teleport-specific; M4B correction 3 says it is the normal exit value of every loop; the C matches M4B.
- `Q-p_map-502-2`: The sweep pointed to M3H8 line 106, which records the reverse walk but not the -1.
- `Q-p_map-618`: described in M3H5 but not flagged as a possible reversal by any document; M3H5's cited lines 615-619 match the C.
- `Q-p_map-625`: M3H3 estimates "about 38.4" (a 22.5° example), M3H5 gives the exact replica value 38.409; no contradiction.
- `Q-p_map-625`: M3H3 cites p_map.c:560-740 for the slide code; P_SlideMove runs to 788.
- `Q-p_map-951`: "behind the trace's start" is the evident consequence of a negative fraction in the C, not a document's wording.
- `Q-p_map-1113`: the tools differ on P_UseSpecialLine's return value: m3h9_cases.py:221 writes "P_UseSpecialLine false" while m3h9_reference.py:207-208 says a player gets the trailing `return true` (value ignored); M3H8's table says "front side: true, nothing happens; back side: false".
- `Q-p_map-1221`: M3H9 cites the function as p_map.c:1206-1229, M3H9B as 1206-1233; the tree has P_RadiusAttack at 1205-1233.
- `Q-p_map-1221`: Example distances differ between documents and tests (M3H9B §7 plan: a barrel 140 units away behind a cell boundary; validation/M3H9B.md observed: Chebyshev distance 136; Rust tests: 113, 127, a 137-unit survivor and a fourth-cell case at 129); these are different scenarios, not a contradiction.
- `Q-p_map-1227`: COMPAT gives no C line.
- `Q-p_map-1290`: COMPATIBILITY's wire-20 text says the item and corpse case "has no C evidence yet"; the M3h8 C room `doorway` (per tools/reference/m3h8_cases.py:213-217) leaves a bonus stuck under a closing door, so a C observation of the item case exists in the tools, though the wire-20 sentence was not updated (the documents were not changed here).
- `Q-p_map-1296`: COMPATIBILITY states the two halves (every linked thing in the block box; any unfitting shootable refuses the step) without naming the unrelated-thing consequence; M3H8 names it.
- `Q-p_maputl-49`: the M3H3 note cites the functions without line numbers; lines here are from the tree.
- `Q-p_maputl-94`: M3H5 cites `P_BoxOnLineSide` at p_maputl.c:110 (the function header); the corner tests are at 139-150.
- `Q-p_maputl-544`: ROADMAP's row cites `p_maputl.c:562` (PIT_AddLineIntercepts); the array is at 544 and the writes at 603-606 and 668-671.
- `Q-p_maputl-668`: the re-add only happens when a cell is visited twice, which the DDA stall row (p_maputl.c:866) describes; the lines beyond the end matter only for the intercept count, since P_TraverseIntercepts stops at `maxfrac` FRACUNIT (p_maputl.c:708-709).
- `Q-p_maputl-866`: M3H5's cited lines (cap 846, step 866-875) match the tree exactly.
- `Q-p_mobj-152`: M3H3 cites p_mobj.c:114 (the function's first line); the test is at 152.
- `Q-p_mobj-152`: No disagreement on the behaviour.
- `Q-p_mobj-152-2`: COMPATIBILITY cites p_map.c:451-517 for P_TryMove.
- `Q-p_mobj-171`: M3H5 cites p_mobj.c:114 (function start); the slide call is at 171.
- `Q-p_mobj-186`: M3H5 cites p_mobj.c:114 (function start).
- `Q-p_mobj-331`: M3B describes the clamping, not the brace.
- `Q-p_mobj-331`: the register builder's observation from the same function: id's comment at p_mobj.c:288-290 says the lost-soul momz negation was "left ... after the setting momz to 0, kinda useless there", but in this text the negation (291-295) comes before the zeroing (297-310), so a skull moving down is negated to positive and not zeroed; the comment describes an older order.
- `Q-p_mobj-479`: M3H4 cites p_mobj.c:480; the function name is at 480 and its signature starts at 479.
- `Q-p_mobj-506`: AGENTS.md states the matching port rule ("raw spawn has no action").
- `Q-p_mobj-758`: the type lookup comes after the netgame and skill filters (p_mobj.c:740-751, verified), so an unknown type whose skill bits exclude the current skill is skipped silently instead of being fatal; M3H9C:202 lists the skill filter before the `doomednum` scan and M3D3I:40 speaks of "enabled unknown types", but no document states the consequence in words.
- `Q-p_mobj-794`: M3H2 says only "signed overflow for THING angle 180"; M3H9 E9 gives the exact range (quotients -4..3 representable, -4 gives INT_MIN) — consistent with the C.
- `Q-p_mobj-900`: both entries guess "missiles aim slightly low"; no document states a player-visible effect.
- `Q-p_mobj-948`: both entries call this design; it is kept because the missile/bullet asymmetry was recorded as a surprise.
- `Q-p_plats-185`: M4A H7 cites p_plats.c:109-120 for the reads; the case itself is 185-195.
- `Q-p_pspr-515`: M4A 3.5.2 says 65 units; its section 14 corrects this to "64 units plus one raw fixed-point unit" (the correction matches the C).
- `Q-p_pspr-515`: The Rust comment cites p_pspr.c:516-517; the comment and calls are at 514-516.
- `Q-p_pspr-528`: the M4a mutation record called a difference of exactly ANG180 impossible; review B5 (M4A section 14) shows it reachable — the review matches the C.
- `Q-p_pspr-542`: COMPATIBILITY's narrower "Nothing on the bite path reads it" matches the C. the rewrite happens before the PST_DEAD test (p_user.c:258), from the C.
- `Q-p_saveg-61`: M5B cites p_saveg.c:59-60; line 59 is blank, 60 is `dest = (player_t *)save_p;` and the memcpy is at 61.
- `Q-p_saveg-329`: M3H7 cites the enum as 331-338 (the members); the enum spans 329-340.
- `Q-p_setup-336`: M3H9 ("recorded, not emulated") and wire 25 (emulated) describe different milestones, not a contradiction.
- `Q-p_setup-479`: M3H3 cites p_maputl.c:376-430 (the unlink and link functions) for both behaviours; the iterator tests are at 481-485 and 520-524.
- `Q-p_sight-320`: The Rust comment at player_episode1_weapons_tests.rs:876 cites p_sight.c:259-270, which is P_CrossBSPNode; the REJECT test is at 313-326 (stale line in a Rust comment).
- `Q-p_spec-329`: M4A §3.13 lists the cut-off as needing a "witness or load rule: < 20 qualifying neighbours per target", while COMPATIBILITY (the later, authoritative home) says it is reproduced.
- `Q-p_spec-329`: The D sweep notes that M4A describes the check as running after "each line (also lines that added nothing)"; the C skips it for a line with no other sector (the `continue` at 348-349), so the M4A wording is slightly off the C.
- `Q-p_spec-382`: The M3h7 note calls it "planned (Stage B)"; COMPATIBILITY records the delivered refusal.
- `Q-p_spec-440`: F cited p_spec.c:434 (the function name line); the comparison is at 441.
- `Q-p_spec-440`: The M3h7 note says "planned (Stage B)"; COMPATIBILITY records it as delivered.
- `Q-p_spec-966`: D cited p_map.c:918-919 and the two-sided test at 921; the call is at 919-920 and the test at 922.
- `Q-p_spec-988`: M5A cites p_switch.c:162-169 for the duplicate-button test (actual 164-171) and p_switch.c:212-213 for the clearing (actual 211-212).
- `Q-p_spec-1187`: G1a calls the status "reproduced as a load rule"; it is a refusal at load.
- `Q-p_spec-1343`: The comment in crates/doom-render/tests/load.rs:96-100 says the C "`I_Error`s on the 65th"; M3H6:207 and M3H7:102-103 say "without a bound check", which matches the C.
- `Q-p_switch-113`: F cited p_switch.c:107-148; the episode filter loop runs to 140.
- `Q-p_switch-113`: ROADMAP:907 cites p_switch.c:107-131.
- `Q-p_switch-211`: M5A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md:2021 cites the clearing at p_switch.c:212-213; it is at 211-212.
- `Q-p_switch-228`: The B sweep marks the full-volume consequence as an inference in M5c (1.2.6.3 "(I)"); COMPATIBILITY states it outright and says it was compared on 64 synthetic items and 61 runs.
- `Q-p_switch-652`: The player-side effect in F's first entry (the trace stops with no sfx_noway) comes from PTR_UseTraverse, which calls P_UseSpecialLine for any special line and returns false whatever it returned (p_map.c:1113-1122), not from the return value.
- `Q-p_telept-101`: line citations differ: the tools cite p_enemy.c:327-328 and [VAL-M4B](validation/M4B.md):993 cites p_enemy.c:319-328 for the z assignment, which is at 333 (327-329 is the MF_INFLOAT clear); M4B note line 2095 cites p_telept.c:100-102 for oldz, which is at 101.
- `Q-p_telept-101`: M4B finding 30 cites p_enemy.c:319-328 for both steps and the Rust comments cite 327-328; the INFLOAT clear is at 328 and `z = floorz` at 332-333.
- `Q-p_telept-113`: The M4a note §3.3.1 item 6 says "20 units in front of the landing"; the M4b note corrects it, and the M4b reading matches the C.
- `Q-p_tick-113`: The M3H9C note words the safety reason as "`Z_Free` writes the block header alone"; M3F8 says Z_Free "marks/coalesces zone blocks without releasing the backing heap"; both say the freed thinker's next field survives.
- `Q-p_user-200`: G1a said "the dangling-pointer framing is only in the code comment"; on re-check COMPATIBILITY.md:2276, M4A:1189 and M5B:2806 record it.
- `Q-r_data-299`: The G2 sweep said no document records it; wrong on re-check (M5A finding 40 and §2.3.3).
- `Q-r_data-351`: Line numbers differ between documents: M5A §2.3.3 and hazard 8 cite r_data.c:351-356, finding 32 cites 353-358; the check is at 351 and the return at 355.
- `Q-r_data-394`: [M5A](M5A_REFERENCE_INVESTIGATION.md):995 states the `lump > 0` test but not the lump-0 consequence.
- `Q-r_data-685`: The G2 sweep said no document records it; wrong on re-check (M5A finding 41, validation/M5A.md:551).
- `Q-r_draw-142`: The H2 entry gives commit 700ee5c's `MaskedCompositeColumn` as MASKPAD's status; per M5A item 49(c) and VAL-M5A the MASKPAD read is an R4 flagged column, not that refusal (which is the separate composite-masked row under r_segs.c:181).
- `Q-r_draw-142-2`: one consequence of the same line as the row above, kept separate because what the player sees differs (repetition, not garbage).
- `Q-r_draw-211`: The original's own comments call it broken.
- `Q-r_draw-279`: a comment that contradicts the code; no behavioural effect.
- `Q-r_main-377`: merges pointtoangle2-overwrites-view-origin and pointtoangle2-writes-viewx-viewy.
- `Q-r_segs-81`: merges pixhigh-pixlow-stale-globals, stale-pixhigh-pixlow-globals and the pixhigh part of renderer-limits-refuse-or-drop-silently.
- `Q-r_segs-81`: M5A item 36 (step 2b) says the case is refused with `StaleGlobal`; item 42 and COMPAT say it is reproduced and the refusal is gone (item 42 is later and supersedes 36; both match the C).
- `Q-r_segs-181`: The Rust doc comment crates/doom-render/src/lib.rs:118-119 still says "a declared refusal awaiting the owner"; COMPAT and M5A record the owner's acceptance on 2026-11-16 (the comment is stale, not contrary to the C).
- `Q-r_segs-181`: M5A item 43 corrects an earlier "never terminates" framing.
- `Q-r_segs-422`: The Rust comment cites r_segs.c:443-446; the unassigned branch is 428-446 (443-446 is its `#endif` and `scale2` line).
- `Q-r_segs-632`: VAL-M5A cites r_segs.c:617-633 for the range; the two tests are at 623 and 632.
- `Q-s_sound-330`: The documents differ by date, not in substance: M3D3G says pitch variation is not modelled (pre-M5c headless scope); the M5b documents call the gap declared and open; COMPAT now records it closed by M5c.
- `Q-s_sound-330`: M5C §1.4.1 item 6 notes stale citations in the helpers: tools/reference/m3h9c_stubs.c:16, m5a_stubs.c:28 and m5b_stubs.c:40 say "st_stuff.c:991" (the draw is at 992) and m5b_driver.c:854 says "m_random.c:47-51" (M_Random is at 63-67); helper bytes are not to be changed.
- `Q-s_sound-773`: COMPAT cites s_sound.c:773, 800-806; the formula ends at 807-808.
- `Q-wadread-245`: M5C cites sndserv/wadread.c:209-214 for the not-found path; it is 212-217 (the scan is 206-210).
- `Q-st_stuff-384`: The stale-face example is labelled an inference in M5B.
- `Q-st_stuff-807`: M5B §4 S23 says "41 of the 42 faces" are reachable (all but god mode); M5B §2.7 corrects this to 40 of 42 (the level-4 ouch face is also unreachable), and VAL-M5B step 1 and COMPAT agree with §2.7 (41 with the bespoke cheat scenario). §2.7 matches the C.
- `Q-st_stuff-816`: COMPAT adds that in one state (dead player, attacker retired) the Rust refusal is wider than the original's read.
- `Q-st_stuff-983`: M5B §1.4.13 ("Not needed: ... st_msgcounter") and §3.7 ("st_msgcounter, st_chat, st_oldchat (inert in single player but stateful)" are saved) disagree on whether it is kept; both agree it has no visible effect.
- `Q-tables-1098`: M3H4 cites `P_PathTraverse` at p_maputl.c:632; the function is at 743 (632 is `tracepositive` in PIT_AddThingIntercepts).
- `Q-v_video-51`: M5B §3.8 says "at gamma 0 it leaves the palette unchanged (inference: §1.3.5 is where the gamma table should be cited)", contradicting M5B §1.3.5, §4 S13 and COMPAT; the C (row 0 starts at 1) matches §1.3.5 and S13.
- `Q-w_wad-370`: M5C calls the lookup "case-insensitive", true only of the query side.
- (no row): M5C §1.1.0 cites the Makefile CFLAGS line as Makefile:9, which is correct, and §1.3.0 item 1 as Makefile:8 with CC at :6 and LIBS at :10; the Makefile has CC at :7, CFLAGS at :9 and LIBS at :11. The Makefile is not a behaviour of the game; recorded here for the documentation owner.
